# Project Status

Latest Batch-D12 / V6-B1.1 production-read checkpoint is complete in code. `src/app/useOfficeProductionReads.js` now owns production-task, packing-task, and machine-queue reads through an injectable action factory and React hook, including workshop machine scoping, DTO projection, combined errors, and shell feedback. Production runtime rejects every non-API source while preserving the last known projection as failed/stale instead of reporting a local success. `useOfficeWorkspace` composes the actions and App no longer imports the three list clients directly. Direct coverage proves all-three success, demo fallback, permission denial, production fail-closed behavior, and `BAG-01` query scoping. Full `npm test`, production/packing/workshop checks, build, and desktop/mobile browser checks pass. `App.jsx` is now 9,747 lines. Print/V1 and remaining read/write orchestration, API/CSS decomposition, real infrastructure, devices, evidence, signoff, and release gates remain incomplete.

Last updated: 2026-07-11

Latest API domain-route split: inventory item filtering/pagination and inventory-ledger reads moved into `server/routes/inventoryReadRoutes.mjs`. The explicit handled-result contract keeps `apiServer.mjs` as the dispatcher, and direct item-filter/ledger/non-match coverage now runs before the existing inventory frontend-client regression. Inventory correction draft reads and all inventory writes remain in the API monolith.

Latest API domain-route split: production-task, machine-queue, and packing-task reads moved into `server/routes/productionReadRoutes.mjs`, with direct list/detail/not-found/non-match coverage running alongside the production/packing client regression. Production schedule, reporting, photo, and packing-complete writes remain in the API monolith.

Latest API domain-route split: order-line list and detail reads moved from `server/apiServer.mjs` into `server/routes/orderReadRoutes.mjs`. The route module returns an explicit handled flag, leaving the API server as the dispatcher. Direct list/detail/not-found/non-match coverage now runs with the existing order-pool client regression. Inventory, fulfillment, and statement reads are the next low-risk extraction candidates; writes remain in the monolith.

Latest API-boundary split: strict-auth policy construction, CORS decisions, public-route classification, and JSON-size configuration moved from `server/apiServer.mjs` into `server/apiSecurityPolicy.mjs`. `api-security:check` now combines route-level strict-auth/CORS verification, direct policy checks, and request-body checks. This is an initial extraction only; business routes remain in the API monolith.

Latest API-boundary split: JSON request-body reading moved from `server/apiServer.mjs` into `server/httpJsonBody.mjs`. The module checks declared `Content-Length`, tracks streamed bytes before buffering chunks, returns `413 / REQUEST_BODY_TOO_LARGE` for both limit paths, and returns `400` for invalid JSON. Direct module coverage is now part of `api-security:check`, alongside the existing chunked-request integration regression.

Latest Git-baseline preparation: `.gitignore` excludes runtime evidence, build output, screenshots, and real environment files. The remaining untracked paths are source, scripts, migrations, docs, and CI only. `docs/development/git-baseline-staging-plan.zh-CN.md` groups those paths by runtime/data, frontend, documentation, and automation with verification commands. No files have been staged, committed, or pushed; owner review is still required before establishing the actual Git baseline.

Latest frontend seed-credential hardening: the browser no longer embeds prototype passwords. `POST /api/auth/prototype-login` accepts only a selected seed user ID in the non-strict local prototype profile, while strict authentication always rejects it with `AUTH_SEED_LOGIN_DISABLED`; real account login remains password-based. The route is documented in OpenAPI and covered for local success plus strict rejection. A production-build scan confirms no seed passwords appear in `dist`.

Latest API-client consolidation: `src/services/officeApiClientCore.js` now provides base-URL resolution, Bearer / operator headers, JSON parsing, standard API errors, and strict-mode write blocking for every office API client. Inventory, master-data import, production / packing, print, orders, fulfillment, todos, statements, raw material, attachments, driver/mobile, and V1 status all reuse the same request boundary. Domain URL construction, payload shaping, response mapping, attachment URL handling, and fallback behavior remain local. Core and all affected client suites pass; static scan finds zero client-local `request*Api` functions.

Latest SQL-parameterization update: master-data review/import, production schedule/packing, raw-material inbound/supplier-statement, statement export, printer-device field test, payment, driver, order-pool, inventory, runtime identity, order, fulfillment, statement, print, attachment, and reservation-release repositories now build `{ text, values }` queries. Static scan finds zero `sqlLiteral(...)` repository builders and zero synchronous repository `psql` calls; repository-specific checks assert business content and apostrophes are absent from SQL text. Docker remains unavailable for real PostgreSQL live integration, so this is not a live-database sign-off.

Latest SQL-parameterization batch: the order-confirmation and fulfillment-action transactions now build `{ text, values }` through `server/postgresSqlParameters.mjs`. Order, line, price, inventory reservation / ledger, fulfillment, print-record, exception, todo, and operation-log values no longer appear in SQL text; the shared pool receives bind values within the same transaction. Repository checks now assert that apostrophes and business content are absent from generated SQL and present only in `values`. Remaining legacy repositories still require staged parameterization, and Docker remains unavailable for real PostgreSQL live integration.

Latest PostgreSQL migration batch: API startup now exposes a `ready` promise and waits for persistent state before serving requests; the executable entrypoint waits for that readiness before binding its port. Payment records use pooled parameterized queries. The remaining attachment metadata, print-device, print-job, and printer-device field-test repositories now also use the shared `pg` pool and awaited routes; synchronous readiness checks retain only the startup-loaded workspace projection. All 29 identified high-risk repositories are now off synchronous `psql`, and `server/*Repository.mjs` no longer contains `spawnSync` database access. Validation passed: async-startup, pool, attachment / print repository checks, print queue and field-QA routes, API/security, OpenAPI, and build checks. Docker remains unavailable for real PostgreSQL live integration; legacy string-built SQL still requires a separate parameterization phase.

Latest verification-command update: `npm run lint` now checks frontend, server, and scripts for blocking static issues, while `npm run check:core` aggregates API structure/security/startup, every frontend API-client regression, database schema/pool, all migrated repository checks, and the production build; `npm test` runs both. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `npm ci` and `npm test` on push and pull requests with read-only permissions. The lint baseline has zero errors and visible unused-variable warnings. This is not yet a full suite: type checking, formatting enforcement, browser smoke coverage, real PostgreSQL, object storage, printing, and field-device checks remain separate.

Latest audit and strict-write update: all audited frontend business writes now fail closed when the browser runs in server-required mode. This includes orders, order adjustments, inventory correction drafts, fulfillment, todo handling, statements, attachments, print batches, master-data import plans/executions, production/packing, printer field tests, and driver delivery actions. A network failure returns `api_error` / `blocked`, so the page does not apply a local business projection. Read-only lists and historical downloads retain explicit prototype/read fallback behavior; V1 status actions already return `blocked=true` on their local fallback path. Validation passed: the affected frontend-client checks, build, API/security, database-pool/migration, V1 runner, and dependency audit. The audit record is `docs/development/code-audit-2026-07-10.zh-CN.md`; the data-layer risk identified in that audit began with 28 synchronous PostgreSQL repositories, while the current count is recorded in the latest migration update above.

Latest PostgreSQL migration batch: order-line quantity adjustment, order-line void, inventory-reservation release, and fulfillment-action transactions now use the shared `pg` pool client, joining the already-migrated order-confirmation transaction. API routes and repository / live-check callers now await the transaction before responding or reading the next state. The batch removes four synchronous `psql` repositories and their connection-string command arguments; 24 repository paths still use synchronous `psql`, and all migrated SQL remains legacy string-built SQL pending the separate parameterization phase. Validation passed: the four transaction checks, fulfillment/driver frontend checks, API/security, pool, build, syntax, and diff checks. Docker remains unavailable for rerunning the real PostgreSQL live integration suite.

Latest PostgreSQL access-layer update: the order-confirmation transaction repository now uses the shared `pg` pool client instead of synchronous `psql`. Its transaction runs on one leased connection, rolls back on failure, releases the connection, and no longer exposes the connection string through a child-process argument. Validation passed: `npm run db-client:check`, `npm run order-confirmation-transaction:check`, `npm run api:check`, `npm run db:check`, `npm run build`, and `git diff --check`. This is the first migration only: 28 repositories still use synchronous CLI helpers, and the existing SQL-string builders still need a staged parameterized-query migration; Docker is still required to rerun the live PostgreSQL integration check.

Latest browser-session storage update: frontend access tokens now use tab-scoped `sessionStorage`, not `localStorage`; the legacy `erp.seedAuthSession.v1` local-storage key is cleared whenever browser auth storage is resolved. This reduces persistent-token exposure on shared devices while preserving a same-tab session restore. Validation passed: `npm run auth:check`, `npm run order-api:check`, `npm run build`, and `git diff --check`. This is still not HttpOnly-cookie authentication or a production identity provider; runtime account persistence, revocation, refresh-token handling, and production login UX remain open.

Latest server-authoritative order-confirmation update: office order confirmation now uses the backend transaction as the only production source of truth. A verified API confirmation refreshes order-pool, inventory, fulfillment, and todo projections; API denial or unavailability leaves the draft unconfirmed and creates no local order, reservation, fulfillment, or todo. Local confirmation remains only for the explicit non-strict prototype fallback. The fulfillment list response now includes customer, order-line, package, and inventory-source fields needed for the refresh projection. Validation passed: `npm run order-api:check`, `npm run fulfillment-api:check`, `npm run api:check`, `npm run api-security:check`, `npm run build`, and `git diff --check`. This closes the duplicate frontend confirmation path, but other write actions still need the same production single-source conversion, and the project still lacks production identity persistence, PostgreSQL, object storage, field devices, evidence, and signoff.

Latest API security-boundary update: the Node API now has an explicit strict authentication profile for production (`ERP_AUTH_MODE=strict` or `NODE_ENV=production`). Strict startup fails without `ERP_AUTH_SECRET`; business routes require a verified bearer session; legacy `seed:<user>` tokens, `x-erp-user-id`, `x-erp-action-permissions`, and the implicit office-user fallback are disabled. Seed-user login is disabled by default, CORS accepts only configured `ERP_CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, JSON requests are capped at 24 MiB by default, and the two closeout CLI reports redact generated artifact paths. The generated production env template and secure-draft setup now include strict mode, secret, CORS, and JSON-limit variables. Local prototype compatibility remains available only outside strict mode. Validation passed: `npm run api-security:check`, `npm run api:check`, `npm run auth:check`, `npm run v1-print-chain-closeout:check`, `npm run v1-driver-real-device-closeout:check`, `npm run v1-production-env-template:check`, and `npm run v1-production-env-setup:check`. This closes API identity spoofing, permissive CORS, unbounded JSON body, and closeout-path disclosure risks; it does not provide production runtime accounts, PostgreSQL, object storage, printer / CUPS configuration, driver phones, real-order pilot evidence, onsite evidence, owner signoff, or V1/V2 boundary confirmation, so V1 remains blocked.

Latest V1 production PostgreSQL restore-isolation hardening update: `scripts/run-v1-production-postgres-backup-restore-check.mjs` now treats restore validation separation as a physical target check, not just raw connection-string inequality. The runner parses standard PostgreSQL URLs and `host` / `port` / `dbname` keyword connection strings, then blocks restore reset whenever the production source and restore-validation target resolve to the same host / port / database, even if usernames, passwords, query parameters, or application names differ. If either connection string cannot be safely parsed, the restore validation is blocked rather than resetting an ambiguous database. The generated production env template and go-live runbook now state that the restore-validation database must use a different host/port/database from the production source. Latest V1 production object-storage governance update: added `scripts/run-v1-production-object-storage-governance-check.mjs` and `npm run v1-production-object-storage-governance:check`, and narrowed `scripts/run-v1-production-object-storage-preflight.mjs` back to live object probes only. The preflight now writes, reads, signed-URL reads, statement-export writes, and cleans diagnostic objects; bucket governance is no longer a preflight blocking criterion. The governance runner uses the secure production env to read attachment and statement-export bucket metadata through signed S3-compatible bucket subresource requests. It checks versioning, lifecycle rules, server-side encryption, and bucket-policy readability without writing any object. Versioning / lifecycle / encryption are blocking for the automation report; bucket-policy readability is a non-blocking warning because some providers use IAM / RAM / CAM policies rather than a readable bucket policy. Reports are redacted and do not print endpoints, buckets, access keys, secrets, signed requests, raw bucket policy, response bodies, object keys, signed URLs, or payloads. `scripts/run-v1-production-persistence-evidence.mjs` now includes governance as a separate `production-object-storage-governance-check` stage after the object-storage live preflight, so first-stage persistence evidence no longer treats write/read/delete alone as complete object-storage readiness. The go-live runbook and generator now include the standalone governance command. Latest raw-material margin-review update: raw-material costing has moved from pending margin snapshots into a first V1 reviewed internal margin report boundary. `POST /api/raw-material-inbounds/{rawMaterialInboundId}/review-margin-snapshot` is now available behind `raw_material.margin.review`; finance / management can review existing `RMMG-*` margin snapshots into `RMMR-*` internal margin reports, while warehouse / office users are denied. Review requires a snapshot and complete linked order revenue; missing snapshots, missing sales amount, and duplicate reviews are blocked with stable business errors. A successful review updates the inbound, drafts, confirmations, loss calibrations, issue records, consumption records, and margin snapshots to `毛利已复核/报表可用` / `已财务复核/报表可用`, with `marginEffect=reviewed_margin_report_snapshot`. This remains an internal finance margin report: it does not write customer statements, does not register collection or final customer settlement, and still does not treat machine cycle counts as qualified output. The frontend API client, local fallback, office `原材料` page action, OpenAPI path / schema, permissions, and raw-material API checks now cover this boundary. Latest raw-material margin-snapshot update: raw-material costing has moved from loss-calibrated material cost snapshots into a first V1 order-margin snapshot boundary. `POST /api/raw-material-inbounds/{rawMaterialInboundId}/generate-margin-snapshot` is now available behind `raw_material.margin.snapshot`; finance / management can turn existing `RMCL-*` loss calibrations into `RMMG-*` margin snapshots, while warehouse / office users are denied. The snapshot reads linked order-line sales amount, calibrated material cost, gross profit, and gross margin rate, then updates the inbound, drafts, confirmations, loss calibrations, issue records, and consumption records to `毛利快照待复核` / `已生成/待财务复核`. Missing sales amount is kept as a review warning instead of being fabricated. Latest raw-material production-task matching update: raw-material machine-side issue now has a first V1 production-task-aware validation layer. `issue-to-machine` still allows an empty `productionTaskId`, but the resulting `RMI-ISS-*` / `RMI-SPLIT-*` records and the inbound summary are explicitly marked `未关联生产任务`, so this path remains traceability-only until the task is filled before cost allocation. When a `productionTaskId` is provided, the backend now requires that the task exists, the issue machine matches the production task machine, and bag-body material color matches the order line bag color; unknown tasks, machine mismatches, and obvious fabric / nonwoven color mismatches return stable 422 business errors instead of falling through to local success. The office `原材料` page refreshes production tasks on entry, defaults compatible bag-body materials to a same-color non-silk production task such as `PT-ORD-0629-003-01`, shows `任务匹配`, and no longer falls back locally for backend 4xx validation errors. This is still not cost allocation, loss calculation, or finished-goods output: it only makes the material issue trace point precise enough for later costing. Latest raw-material split / partial-consumption update: raw-material machine-side flow now has a first V1 split-roll and measured partial-consumption loop for weighted rolls. `issue-to-machine` accepts an `issuedWeightKg` lower than the available roll weight, creates `RMI-SPLIT-*`, leaves the source roll in `可用` with the remaining weight, creates a machine-side child roll such as `RM-...-S01`, and writes source/child linkage on the issue record. `confirm-consumption` now accepts a lower `consumedWeightKg` for machine-side weighted rolls, creates `RMI-CONS-*`, records consumed and remaining machine-side weight, and keeps the child roll in `机边领用` with `部分消耗/机边` until the rest is consumed or returned as leftover. Issue records now keep source-roll remaining weight and machine-side remaining weight as separate fields (`remainingWeightKg` vs `remainingMachineSideWeightKg`) so warehouse and costing review do not confuse original stock remainder with machine-side remainder. The office `原材料` page now shows `部分领料`, `部分消耗`, split counts, formatted split records, and machine-side remaining weight. This is still traceability / inventory-state only: it does not create finished goods, does not use machine count as qualified quantity, does not allocate material cost, does not calculate loss, and does not compute margin. Latest raw-material leftover review update: machine-side leftovers now have a first V1 return-to-available review loop. `rawMaterialInboundRepository` supports `review-leftover` / `复核余料可用` in local JSON and PostgreSQL payload modes; the API exposes `POST /api/raw-material-inbounds/{rawMaterialInboundId}/review-leftover` behind `raw_material.leftover.review`. Only rolls / pieces in `余料待复核` can be reviewed. A successful review creates `RMI-LREV-*`, records reviewed weight / quantity, review location, reviewer, and time, updates the matching return and issue records to `余料已复核/可用`, and moves the roll / piece back to `可用`. Latest raw-material consumption / leftover update: raw-material inbound now has a first V1 follow-up loop after machine-side issue. `rawMaterialInboundRepository` supports `confirm-consumption` / `确认消耗` and `return-leftover` / `余料退回` in local JSON and PostgreSQL payload modes; the API exposes `POST /api/raw-material-inbounds/{rawMaterialInboundId}/confirm-consumption` behind `raw_material.consumption.confirm` and `POST /api/raw-material-inbounds/{rawMaterialInboundId}/return-leftover` behind `raw_material.leftover.return`. Only `机边领用` rolls / pieces can be acted on. Full-roll / full-piece consumption creates `RMI-CONS-*`, moves the roll / piece to `已消耗`, and updates the issue record to `已确认消耗`. Leftover return creates `RMI-RET-*`, moves the roll / piece to `余料待复核`, and does not automatically restore available inventory. Both actions explicitly avoid finished-goods output creation, machine-count-as-qualified-quantity, cost allocation, loss calibration, and margin calculation. The office `原材料` page now shows per-roll `确认消耗` and `余料退回`, plus issue / consumption / leftover record counts and formatted records. Latest raw-material supplier-statement review update: supplier monthly statement precheck results can now be saved as durable manual review drafts. `server/rawMaterialSupplierStatementReviewRepository.mjs` supports local JSON and PostgreSQL payload persistence through `raw_material_supplier_statement_reviews`, and the API exposes `GET /api/raw-material-supplier-statement-reviews`, `POST /api/raw-material-supplier-statement-reviews`, and `POST /api/raw-material-supplier-statement-reviews/{reviewId}/confirm-review` under the existing raw-material review permission. The office `原材料` page now refreshes the backend review-draft list, shows `月结复核草稿`, and can save an uploaded precheck result for later human review; the UI states that these drafts do not write inventory, create payables, or confirm payment. The repository is also included in the unified V1 persistence profile and system V1 readiness gate, so production profile checks now cover 29 PostgreSQL repository defaults plus 2 object-storage defaults, and `/api/system/v1-readiness` checks 31 persistence objects. Latest raw-material supplier-statement import update: the office raw-material page now has the first supplier monthly statement Excel precheck path. `src/domain/rawMaterialSupplierStatementImport.js` detects Baihou, Beichen, and generic supplier statement templates, normalizes rows before matching ERP raw-material inbounds, splits Baihou `重1`-`重5` into roll-level rows, treats Baihou footer tube / return / arrears rows as review-only adjustments, parses Beichen shifted return sections, and uses Beichen `批号` as a strong roll key. Supplier document numbers remain optional: when a statement row has no supplier number, the precheck can only produce a candidate match from supplier, spec, color, total / roll weight, price, and amount evidence, not an automatic confirmation. The `原材料` page now exposes `上传月结 Excel`, shows adapter summary, matched / candidate / unmatched rows, adjustments, and issues; import precheck does not write inventory, payable, or payment records. Validation passed: `node --check src/domain/rawMaterialSupplierStatementImport.js`, `node --check scripts/check-raw-material-supplier-statement-import.mjs`, `npm run raw-material-supplier-statement-import:check`, `npm run raw-material-inbound-page:check`, `npm run raw-material-inbound-api:check`, `npm run seed:check`, `npm run auth:check`, `npm run build`, and in-app browser checks at `1280px` / `390px` with screenshots `screenshots/p0-raw-material-supplier-statement-import-1280.png` and `screenshots/p0-raw-material-supplier-statement-import-390.png`. Latest raw-material optional supplier-note-number update: real supplier photos showed that some incoming raw-material delivery/sales notes do not have a supplier document number. The ERP now treats `deliveryNoteNo` as optional in the raw-material inbound prototype: seed data includes a supplier with no document number, the office `原材料` page displays `供应商未提供单号 / ERP 入库单号`, detail text explains that supplier document number is an external reconciliation clue while ERP inbound ID and roll/piece IDs are the stable internal keys, and action toasts fall back to the ERP inbound ID instead of rendering a blank supplier number. Requirements and decisions now state that supplier number should be recorded when present and left blank when absent, while monthly reconciliation without supplier number should fall back to ERP inbound ID / source photo plus supplier, date/time, specification, color, piece/roll count, roll sequence, per-roll weight, unit price, and amount. Validation passed: `npm run raw-material-inbound-page:check`, `npm run raw-material-inbound-api:check`, `npm run seed:check`, `npm run auth:check`, `npm run build`, `git diff --check`, and in-app browser verification on the local fallback preview showing `供应商未提供单号 / RMI-0704-001`. Latest raw-material inbound API update: raw-material inbound has moved from a local frontend loop to a first API / persistence boundary. `server/rawMaterialInboundRepository.mjs` now supports `local_json` persistence and PostgreSQL payload persistence through the new `raw_material_inbounds` table, with action logs for delivery-note review, roll-label printing, label attachment confirmation, and exception marking. `GET /api/raw-material-inbounds`, detail read, and action routes are wired into the API, OpenAPI, auth permissions, V1 persistence profile, and `/api/system/v1-readiness`; production profile checks now include `rawMaterialInboundRepository=postgres`. The office `原材料` page now reads and writes through the backend when available, shows `后端 API 已同步`, and only falls back to local rules for API unavailability, while permission errors remain blocking. Validation passed: `npm run raw-material-inbound-api:check`, `npm run raw-material-inbound-page:check`, `npm run api:validate-openapi`, `npm run api:check`, `npm run auth:check`, `npm run db:check`, `npm run db:migrate:dry`, `npm run v1-persistence-profile:check`, `npm run v1-readiness:check`, `npm run v1-production-profile-live:check`, `npm run v1-completion-snapshot:check`, `npm run build`, and in-app browser desktop / 390px checks with screenshots `screenshots/p0-raw-material-inbound-api-1280.png` and `screenshots/p0-raw-material-inbound-api-390.png`. This raises raw materials / cost / margin to a backend-verifiable inbound boundary, but V1 remains blocked until real OCR, real label printer / label paper, phone scan / signed-note field acceptance, supplier Excel import adapters, raw-material issue / consumption, cost allocation, loss calibration, and margin reporting are implemented and verified.

Latest V1 system-persistence gate update: the system readiness gate now includes `runtimeIdentityRepository`. The unified V1 persistence profile already moved imported-employee runtime users and seed-session revocations to PostgreSQL in production mode, but `/api/system/v1-readiness` did not list that repository in any persistence group. That created a false-coverage risk for employee password state, lockout, password expiry, and session revocation durability. The repository is now part of the evidence / audit / print / master-data / identity group, the readiness runner preserves group-level repository details, and the production-profile live check now expects 29 checked persistence objects with `runtimeIdentityRepository=postgres` and production ready. Validation passed: `node --check server/apiServer.mjs`, `node --check scripts/run-v1-readiness-check.mjs`, `node --check scripts/check-v1-readiness-runner.mjs`, `node --check scripts/check-v1-production-profile-live.mjs`, `node --check scripts/check-api-skeleton.mjs`, `npm run api:check`, `npm run v1-readiness:check`, and `npm run v1-production-profile-live:check`. This tightens release-gate coverage only; V1 remains blocked until real production configuration, printer / driver-phone field evidence, business pilots, signoff, and V1/V2 boundary confirmation are complete.

Latest raw-material inbound workflow update: real fabric / nonwoven raw-material supplier delivery-note photos have now confirmed the first raw-material receiving loop. Requirements now define phone photo upload of the raw-material supplier delivery/sales note that arrives with the goods, OCR as prefill only, customer-service / office review against the physical supplier label, one system label per roll/piece, label printing as a pending-attachment state, physical label attachment to the matching roll, and phone scan / signed-note upload before a roll becomes available raw-material inventory. This inbound document is explicitly separate from finished-goods outbound / customer delivery notes, and supplier document numbers are optional because some suppliers do not provide them. Supplier monthly statement upload is now documented as a later reconciliation loop that matches supplier rows against ERP-confirmed inbound records and highlights missing inbound, supplier omissions, duplicates, specification / color / weight / unit-price / amount differences; reconciliation confirmation remains separate from payment.

Latest supplier-statement sample analysis update: three real Excel workbooks from two suppliers were inspected. The Baihou first-half / second-half June files are single-sheet `对账单` workbooks with fixed columns for document date, document number, customer, product/spec, color, roll count, `重1`-`重5`, total weight, unit price, and amount; they include formulas for amount, per-roll weights that match total weight, and footer rows for current-period totals, tube/core deductions, and historical arrears. The Beichen April file contains `每日发货明细` and `每日发货统计`; the detail sheet has positive shipment rows with `批号` as a strong roll/batch key and a separate shifted return section near the bottom, while the statistics sheet is only a validation summary. Requirements now call for supplier-specific import adapters that normalize both formats before matching ERP inbound records. This makes automatic supplier reconciliation feasible, but implementation still needs adapter code, UI, more supplier templates, paper-tube / adjustment configuration, and field verification.

Latest release-candidate env-file gate update: the V1 release-candidate check now folds the real production env-file safety audit into the production environment gate. When `--env-file` is provided, `scripts/run-v1-release-candidate-check.mjs` runs `scripts/run-v1-production-env-file-audit.mjs`, writes a redacted `envFileAudit` object into the JSON result, and adds a "生产 env 文件安全审计" section to the Markdown report. The release gate count remains `4`, but the production environment gate now requires both variable preflight and env-file audit readiness. A `.env.example`, docs path, git-tracked file, non-ignored workspace file, or file with unresolved placeholders keeps the release candidate blocked even if variable preflight is otherwise `9/9`. Validation passed: `node --check scripts/run-v1-release-candidate-check.mjs`, `node --check scripts/check-v1-release-candidate-check.mjs`, `npm run v1-release-candidate:check`, `npm run v1-production-env-file-audit:check`, and `npm run v1-production-env-preflight:check`. This closes a release-candidate false-ready risk only; V1 remains blocked until real production configuration, real print / driver-device field evidence, workflow pilots, signoff, and V1/V2 boundary confirmation are complete.

Latest V1 production-env file audit update: real production env files now have a separate redacted safety audit before preflight. `scripts/run-v1-production-env-file-audit.mjs` accepts one or more `--env-file` paths and checks that each file is not a docs/template file, is not tracked by git, is inside a git-ignored path or outside the workspace, has no unresolved `<REPLACE_WITH_...>` / `<OPTIONAL_...>` placeholders, and only reports duplicate variables / broad file permissions as warnings. It never prints raw env values, comments, connection strings, object-storage endpoints or buckets, access keys, secret keys, command paths, command args, spool paths, tokens, or payloads. `package.json` now exposes `npm run v1-production-env-file-audit:check`; `.gitignore` now ignores common local env file patterns; `docs/development/v1-production.env.example`, `docs/development/v1-go-live-runbook.zh-CN.md`, and `production-env-fill-template.env.example` generation now tell operators to audit the secure env file before running production preflight. Validation passed: `node --check scripts/run-v1-production-env-file-audit.mjs`, `node --check scripts/check-v1-production-env-file-audit.mjs`, and `npm run v1-production-env-file-audit:check`. This closes a secret-handling / handoff-safety gap only; the actual V1 release remains blocked until real PostgreSQL, object storage, CUPS / printers, driver phones, field evidence, signoff, and V1/V2 boundary confirmation are complete.

Latest V1 unblock-plan / env-fill update: the go-live suite and the handoff pack now carry the same "minimum unblock plan" for field execution, and the handoff pack now writes a safe production env fill draft. `scripts/run-v1-go-live-suite.mjs` writes `.erp-local-storage/v1-go-live-suite/v1-unblock-plan.zh-CN.md` plus `.json` before invoking the handoff pack, and `scripts/run-v1-go-live-handoff-pack.mjs` copies those files into `.erp-local-storage/v1-go-live-handoff/v1-unblock-plan.latest.zh-CN.md` plus `.json`, summarizes the phase order in `handoff-summary.zh-CN.md`, and generates `production-env-fill-template.env.example` from the current redacted env fix checklist. The env fill template contains only variable names and `<REPLACE_WITH_...>` placeholders; real values still belong only in a secure untracked env file. The current real suite still has `52` blockers, grouped into six execution phases: production environment / persistence `16`, real print chain `11`, driver real-device acceptance `7`, real business pilot `9`, security / signoff / V1-V2 boundary `8`, and other remaining `1`. The first concrete actions are the unified V1 persistence profile, attachment object storage, statement-export object storage, system-printer command bridge, and system V1 persistence gate. The onsite task-board role inference was also narrowed so "production environment variables" are not incorrectly assigned to workshop just because they contain the word production. Validation passed: `node --check scripts/run-v1-go-live-handoff-pack.mjs`, `node --check scripts/run-v1-go-live-suite.mjs`, `node --check scripts/check-v1-go-live-handoff-pack.mjs`, `node --check scripts/check-v1-go-live-suite.mjs`, `npm run v1-go-live-handoff:check`, `npm run v1-go-live-suite:check`, and `npm run v1-completion-snapshot:check`. This improves the execution path only; the actual release remains blocked at `0/4` gates, `0/34` field evidence, `0/6` signoffs, V1 readiness `78-81%`, and `17` planned V2 differences.

Latest imported-employee lockout / password-expiry update: imported runtime employee accounts now have the first V1 account-security boundary. Five consecutive failed login attempts persist `failedLoginCount`, `lastFailedLoginAt`, and a 15-minute `lockedUntil`; locked accounts return `AUTH_ACCOUNT_LOCKED`, including when the next attempt uses the correct password. Successful password changes now write a 90-day `passwordExpiresAt`, clear failed-login / lockout state, and keep returning sanitized runtime users. When an active password is expired, the next valid login marks the account `password_expired`, sets `mustChangePassword=true`, returns `passwordChangeRequired=true`, and removes business action / button permissions until the employee changes password. Admin temporary-password reissue clears lockout / expiry state while still invalidating old sessions through `sessionVersion`; revoke clears password and lockout fields. The new fields persist through `runtimeIdentityRepository` in local JSON and PostgreSQL `users.metadata_json`. Validation passed: touched-file `node --check`, `npm run runtime-identity:check`, `npm run master-data-import-api:check`, and `npm run auth:check`. Remaining identity boundaries are production deployment, field acceptance, real account migration / operations, broader Excel sample acceptance, field evidence, and signoff.

Latest master-data Excel date normalization update: the master-data precheck now normalizes Excel serial-number dates only for explicit date fields such as `生效日期` and `盘点日期`, returning `YYYY-MM-DD` staged values while leaving quantity, unit price, inventory, machine count, and other numeric fields unchanged. It also accepts common date text forms such as slash, dot, and Chinese year/month/day separators. The external compressed workbook fixture now stores price-table, inventory, and employee-machine dates as numeric Excel serial cells and asserts the staged rows still contain `2026-07-03` / `2026-07-01`. Validation passed: `node --check src/domain/masterDataImportPrecheck.js`, `node --check scripts/check-master-data-import-precheck.mjs`, and `npm run master-data-precheck:check`. This reduces real master-data import risk but does not replace broader real-world Excel sample acceptance, production deployment, field signoff, or full account lockout / password expiry controls.

Latest master-data maintenance page update: the ERP now has an independent `基础资料` main navigation entry for customer records, price snapshots, inventory/spec records, and employee / machine account records. The page supports tabbed search, metrics, detail review, and a first non-writing maintenance-draft flow: users can create `MDM-*` drafts for a selected field/value/reason, but formal master-data writes still go through the existing import confirmation / execution chain. This keeps high-risk price, inventory, and employee data behind review boundaries instead of allowing direct table edits. Validation passed: `npm run master-data-maintenance-page:check`, `npm run build`, and browser checks at `1280px` and `390px`; screenshots are saved as `screenshots/p0-master-data-maintenance-1280.png` and `screenshots/p0-master-data-maintenance-390.png`. Remaining master-data boundaries are broader real-world Excel sample coverage, real data import field acceptance, production deployment, and full account lockout / password expiry controls.

Latest master-data external Excel precheck compatibility update: the master-data workbook reader now prefers the xlsx ZIP central directory instead of relying only on sequential local file headers. It can read externally re-saved workbooks with deflate-compressed entries, local-header zero sizes plus data descriptors, rich-text shared strings, formula cached values, single-quoted XML attributes, value tags with attributes, cells without explicit `r` coordinates, and normalized absolute / `..` workbook relationship targets. `scripts/check-master-data-import-precheck.mjs` now builds a compressed external-style workbook fixture with sharedStrings rich text, omitted cell coordinates in the customer sheet header, and formula cached price / inventory quantities; precheck still produces five valid staged rows. Validation passed: `node --check src/domain/masterDataImportPrecheck.js`, `node --check scripts/check-master-data-import-precheck.mjs`, and `npm run master-data-precheck:check`. Later work added the independent maintenance page; remaining master-data boundaries are broader real-world Excel sample coverage, real data import field acceptance, production deployment, and full account lockout / password expiry controls.

Latest master-data failed-row inline correction update: the master-data import execution list now has a compact `修正字段` editor for failed rows before creating a correction draft. Office / management users can expand an `MDE-*` execution, edit the failed row values inline, reset a row back to its original failed-row values, and then call `POST /api/master-data/import-executions/{executionId}/failed-rows/correction-draft` with `rowCorrections`. Only touched rows count as corrected, so unreviewed failed rows remain `unresolved`; corrected drafts preserve source execution / plan, failed reasons, correction summary, and operation logs, and still do not write formal master data. Latest master-data failed-row correction draft update: import executions with failed rows can now generate a non-writing correction review draft and re-enter the existing confirmation-plan / formal-import flow. `GET /api/master-data/import-review-drafts` lists persisted review drafts, and `POST /api/master-data/import-executions/{executionId}/failed-rows/correction-draft` creates an `MDI-*` draft from an `MDE-*` execution's failed rows while preserving source execution, source plan, failed reasons, correction summary, and operation log. The master-data modal now refreshes saved review drafts when opened, shows the source execution on correction drafts, and exposes `生成修正草稿` beside `下载失败行`; both actions use `master_data.import.plan.create`, while actual writes still require `master_data.import.execute`. Latest imported-employee password policy / admin UI update: master-data employee review now exposes management reset and revoke as visible row actions. The modal shows password state for imported employees (`temporary password awaiting change`, `active password`, or `password revoked`), keeps the visible-once credential panel, displays the V1 password rule, and clears the credential panel after a revoke. The frontend now calls the existing revoke API through `revokeOfficeMasterDataEmployeeAccountPassword`, and `撤销员工密码` is wired to the same management-only password permission as temporary-password issuance. Runtime password changes now enforce the V1 policy: at least 10 characters, at least one letter, at least one number, no whitespace, and no login name / user ID / employee ID substring; policy errors return a `passwordPolicy` object for future employee-facing UI. Latest runtime identity persistence update: imported runtime employee accounts and seed-session revocations now have a dedicated `runtimeIdentityRepository`. The default `local_json` mode persists dynamic users and revoked token `jti` values under `.erp-local-storage/metadata/runtime-identity.json`, so imported employee password changes and `/api/auth/logout` revocations survive API server restart. PostgreSQL mode writes imported runtime users into the `users` table and revoked sessions into the new `seed_session_revocations` table; the unified V1 persistence profile now includes `runtimeIdentityRepositoryOptions`, so the production profile applies PostgreSQL defaults to 27 repository boundaries. Validation passed: `npm run runtime-identity:check`, `npm run master-data-import-api:check`, `npm run api:check`, `npm run auth:check`, `npm run db:check`, `npm run db:migrate:dry`, `npm run db:postgres-live:check`, `npm run api:validate-openapi`, `npm run seed:check`, `npm run v1-persistence-profile:check`, `npm run v1-production-profile-live:check`, `npm run v1-completion-snapshot:check`, `npm run v1-go-live-suite:check`, and `npm run build`. Remaining identity gaps are real production deployment, field acceptance, broader Excel compatibility, independent maintenance pages, and full account lockout / password expiry controls.

Latest imported-employee password reset / revoke session boundary update: imported runtime employee accounts now carry a `sessionVersion` in seed-session tokens. Reissuing a temporary password increments that version, so previously changed-password sessions are rejected with `AUTH_TOKEN_REVOKED`; revoking a password clears the runtime password hash, sets `loginEnabled=false`, marks `passwordStatus=password_revoked`, rejects the active temporary session, and blocks old-password login. `/api/auth/logout` records the current token `jti` for revocation, now backed by the runtime identity repository described above. `POST /api/master-data/employee-account-reviews/{employeeId}/password/revoke` is documented in OpenAPI and exposed through the master-data frontend API client, and the master-data review UI now exposes the revoke button. Validation passed: `npm run master-data-import-api:check`, `npm run api:check`, `npm run api:validate-openapi`, `npm run auth:check`, and `npm run seed:check`. Remaining identity gaps are real production deployment, field acceptance, broader Excel compatibility, independent maintenance pages, and full account lockout / password expiry controls.

Latest imported-employee password-change boundary update: runtime accounts created from master-data employee import now lose business permissions while `mustChangePassword=true`. Temporary-password login returns `passwordChangeRequired=true` with empty action / button permissions, and `POST /api/auth/change-password` verifies the current temporary password, rejects weak / same passwords, clears `mustChangePassword`, marks `passwordStatus=active`, syncs the employee account review row, writes a password-change operation log, and makes the old temporary password invalid. The frontend auth service now exposes the same API, OpenAPI documents the route / permission response fields, and the master-data import API check covers temporary-login permission convergence, invalid password changes, successful first-password change, old-temp-password rejection, and new-password login. Later updates have added runtime identity persistence, stronger V1 password policy, and management reset / revoke UI. Remaining identity gaps are real production deployment, field acceptance, broader Excel compatibility, independent maintenance pages, and full account lockout / password expiry controls.
