Latest V1 field-evidence intake guidance update: the onsite evidence intake pack now writes `intake-rules.zh-CN.md` next to `evidence-items.csv` and `signoff-boundary.csv`. The rules file documents allowed status values, required fields, recommended evidence and timestamp formats, apply / validate / suite commands, and safety boundaries. `scripts/apply-v1-field-evidence-intake.mjs` now adds redacted `fixHint` values to `invalidRows`, so operators can correct missing statuses, missing evidence refs, missing signer / time fields, or sensitive-looking values without seeing or leaking raw refs or signer names. `scripts/run-v1-go-live-handoff-pack.mjs` now copies the rules file into handoff packs, and the generated field-evidence checklist plus go-live runbook now tell operators to read the rules before filling CSVs. Validation passed: `npm run v1-field-evidence-intake:check`, `npm run v1-field-evidence-intake-apply:check`, `npm run v1-go-live-handoff:check`, `npm run v1-go-live-suite:check`, and `npm run v1-field-evidence:check`. The refreshed real suite remains blocked: gates `0/4`, field evidence `0/34`, signoffs `0/6`.

Latest V1 go-live suite canonical latest sync update: `scripts/run-v1-go-live-suite.mjs` now supports `--sync-canonical-latest` plus a test-only `--canonical-root`. When enabled, the suite copies the same-run redacted downstream artifacts into the top-level `.erp-local-storage/v1-onsite-task-board/`, `.erp-local-storage/v1-completion-snapshot/`, `.erp-local-storage/v1-v2-scope-brief/`, `.erp-local-storage/v1-owner-decision-brief/`, `.erp-local-storage/v1-field-evidence-intake/`, and `.erp-local-storage/v1-go-live-handoff/` directories. This prevents different `latest` entrypoints from showing different timestamps or blocker counts after a suite run. The sync copies reports and handoff packs only; it does not mutate field evidence, promote pending evidence, or override release gates. When syncing the field-evidence intake pack, it preserves existing `evidence-items.csv`, `signoff-boundary.csv`, and `filled-manifest.draft.json` so onsite operator edits are not overwritten. The generated go-live runbook now recommends the flag, and validation passed with `npm run v1-go-live-suite:check`. The real current suite was refreshed with canonical sync and remains blocked: gates `0/4`, field evidence `0/34`, signoffs `0/6`, onsite tasks `52`, and planned V2 differences `17`.

Latest V1 production-env placeholder hardening update: `scripts/run-v1-production-env-preflight.mjs` now treats unresolved template placeholders such as `<REPLACE_WITH_...>`, `<OPTIONAL_...>`, and values containing `REPLACE_WITH_` as unconfigured instead of valid production settings. The preflight report only exposes a `placeholderValueCount` plus variable-name statuses, not placeholder text or real values. `scripts/check-v1-production-env-preflight.mjs` now covers the common failure mode where an operator copies the template and uncomments placeholder values without replacing them. The generated runbook now explicitly says placeholders are treated as missing and operators must not only uncomment template rows. Validation passed: `npm run v1-production-env-preflight:check` and `npm run v1-release-candidate:check`. Current V1 status remains blocked; this hardening prevents a false-ready env preflight but does not provide real PostgreSQL, object storage, printer bridge, CUPS, field evidence, or signoff.

Latest V1 field-evidence signoff/boundary backfill update: the V1 onsite intake flow now has a structured CSV path for required signoffs and V1/V2 boundary confirmation. `scripts/run-v1-field-evidence-intake-pack.mjs` writes `signoff-boundary.csv` next to `evidence-items.csv`; `scripts/apply-v1-field-evidence-intake.mjs` accepts `--signoff-boundary-csv` and can merge `signed` / `accepted` signoffs plus `confirmed` V1/V2 boundary rows into a new draft field-evidence manifest while requiring signer / signed-at and confirmed-by / confirmed-at values. `scripts/run-v1-go-live-suite.mjs` accepts `--field-evidence-signoff-boundary-csv`, and final go/no-go runs still need `--refresh-release-candidate` so the release gates read the draft. `scripts/run-v1-go-live-handoff-pack.mjs` now copies `signoff-boundary.csv` into the handoff pack under `field-evidence-intake/`. Validation passed: `npm run v1-field-evidence-intake-apply:check`, `npm run v1-field-evidence-intake:check`, `npm run v1-field-evidence:check`, `npm run v1-go-live-handoff:check`, and `npm run v1-go-live-suite:check`. The current refreshed suite remains blocked: gates `0/4`, field evidence `0/34`, signoffs `0/6`, onsite tasks `52`, and planned V2 differences `17`.

Latest V1 go-live handoff V1/V2 brief update: `scripts/run-v1-go-live-handoff-pack.mjs` now includes the generated V1/V2 scope brief in the handoff pack, instead of only copying the source scope document. It accepts `--v1-v2-scope-brief-json` and `--v1-v2-scope-brief-markdown`, auto-discovers `.erp-local-storage/v1-v2-scope-brief/latest.*` when present, copies them as `v1-v2-scope-brief.latest.zh-CN.md/json`, and summarizes V1 must-continue work, V2 difference counts, V2 categories, and owner review rules in `handoff-summary.zh-CN.md`. The go-live suite now passes the same-run `v1V2ScopeBrief` outputs into the handoff pack so the suite handoff directory cannot silently pick up an older default brief. Validation passed: `npm run v1-go-live-handoff:check` and `npm run v1-go-live-suite:check`. The current release state remains blocked: gates `0/4`, field evidence `0/34`, signoffs `0/6`.

Latest V1/V2 scope brief update: `scripts/run-v1-v2-scope-brief.mjs` now generates a redacted owner-facing V1/V2 difference brief under `.erp-local-storage/v1-v2-scope-brief/`. It reads `docs/development/v1-v2-scope.zh-CN.md` plus the completion snapshot, then writes `latest.zh-CN.md` / `latest.json` with V1 must-continue items, planned V2 differences, V2 category grouping, and an explicit owner review rule that production configuration, real devices, field evidence, and signoff cannot be moved to V2. `package.json` exposes `npm run v1-v2-scope-brief:check`, covering blocked and ready summaries, missing source handling, text output, V2 categories, generated files, and redaction. The go-live suite now generates this brief as the `v1V2ScopeBrief` step. The current run remains blocked, with `17` planned V2 differences across `7` categories while V1 gates remain `0/4`, field evidence `0/34`, and signoffs `0/6`.

Latest V1 go-live suite CSV backfill update: `scripts/run-v1-go-live-suite.mjs` now accepts `--field-evidence-intake-csv` and `--field-evidence-draft-output`. When a field owner fills `evidence-items.csv`, the suite first applies those rows into a new draft field-evidence manifest, keeps the source manifest untouched, and then generates the onsite task board, completion snapshot, owner decision brief, field-evidence intake pack, and handoff pack from the draft. If the draft is used without `--refresh-release-candidate`, the suite marks `releaseCandidateRefreshRecommendedAfterDraftApply=true` and warns that the existing release-candidate JSON may still reflect the old evidence state. For final go/no-go, the runbook now requires the suite command to include `--refresh-release-candidate` so release gates are recalculated from the draft manifest. `npm run v1-go-live-suite:check` covers both paths: the no-refresh path warns about stale gates, and the refresh path proves the release-candidate field-evidence summary moves from `0/34` to `1/34` while release gates remain blocked at `0/4`, so it cannot accidentally declare V1 complete.

Latest V1 go-live suite update: `scripts/run-v1-go-live-suite.mjs` now orchestrates the current release-candidate report plus the filled field-evidence manifest into one redacted local suite under `.erp-local-storage/v1-go-live-suite/`. The suite generates the onsite task board, completion snapshot, owner decision brief, field-evidence intake pack, and go-live handoff pack, then writes `latest.zh-CN.md` / `latest.json` as the single current-state entrypoint. `package.json` exposes `npm run v1-go-live-suite:check`, covering blocked release orchestration, downstream file generation, owner-brief copying into the handoff pack, missing release-candidate handling, and redaction. Running it against the current local release candidate still reports `blocked_go_live_suite_written`: release gates are `0/4`, onsite tasks are `52`, P0 prototype/code is `97-98%`, V1 go-live readiness is `76-79%`, field evidence is `0/34`, signoffs are `0/6`, and planned V2 differences are `17`. This is a command-chain and handoff improvement only; it does not make pending field evidence pass or change the V1 completion criteria.

Latest V1 owner-decision brief update: `scripts/run-v1-owner-decision-brief.mjs` now turns the generated V1 completion snapshot into a concise owner-facing decision package under `.erp-local-storage/v1-owner-decision-brief/`. It answers whether V1 can be declared complete, lists completed / unfinished items, summarizes blocker groups and next actions, and carries the planned V2 differences without printing raw evidence refs, signer names, real env values, command paths, spool paths, or secrets. `package.json` exposes `npm run v1-owner-decision-brief:check`, covering blocked and ready briefs, generated files, missing snapshot handling, V2 differences, and redaction. The go-live handoff pack now auto-copies `v1-owner-decision-brief.latest.zh-CN.md/json` when present. Running the brief against the current local snapshot still reports blocked: V1 cannot be declared complete, P0 prototype/code is `97-98%`, V1 go-live readiness is `76-79%`, release gates are `0/4`, field evidence is `0/34`, signoffs are `0/6`, and onsite tasks remain `52`.

Latest V1 field-evidence intake-apply update: `scripts/apply-v1-field-evidence-intake.mjs` now turns filled intake CSV rows into a new field-evidence manifest draft without mutating the source manifest. Rows are applied only when `onsiteStatus` is explicit, `passed` / `accepted` rows require `onsiteEvidenceRef`, and sensitive-looking values are rejected. The command writes `.erp-local-storage/v1-field-evidence-intake/filled-manifest.draft.json` by default and keeps stdout redacted. `package.json` exposes `npm run v1-field-evidence-intake-apply:check`, covering partial draft writes, source preservation, sensitive-row rejection, required `onsiteStatus`, and redacted output. Applying the current empty intake CSV writes a blocked draft with `0` applied rows and `34` skipped rows, so the current V1 evidence state remains `0/34` evidence and `0/6` signoffs.

Latest frontend build-splitting update: `vite.config.mjs` now defines Vite `manualChunks` for React, Ant Design icons, ERP pages, ERP runtime services/state, domain rules, and seed data. The first split of `state` and `services` produced a Rollup circular chunk warning, so those modules are now grouped into one `erp-runtime` chunk. The latest `npm run build` succeeds without the previous `Some chunks are larger than 500 kB` warning; the largest production chunk is now about `273 kB`.

Latest V1 field-evidence intake update: `scripts/run-v1-field-evidence-intake-pack.mjs` now creates a redacted onsite evidence intake pack under `.erp-local-storage/v1-field-evidence-intake/`. It reads the field-evidence manifest and can include the current release-candidate report, onsite task board, and completion snapshot, then writes a summary, machine-readable manifest, CSV, signoff / V1-V2 boundary sheet, and six per-evidence-group Markdown intake sheets. `package.json` exposes `npm run v1-field-evidence-intake:check`, covering blocked and ready packs, per-group files, CSV generation, optional report inputs, explicit missing input errors, and redaction. The go-live handoff pack now auto-copies the intake pack into `field-evidence-intake/` when present. Running the intake pack against the current local manifest still reports `blocked`: evidence groups `0/6`, required evidence `0/34`, signoffs `0/6`, release candidate `0/4`, and V1 go-live readiness `76-79%`.

Latest V1 completion-snapshot update: `scripts/run-v1-completion-snapshot.mjs` now creates a redacted current-state snapshot under `.erp-local-storage/v1-completion-snapshot/`. It reads the release-candidate JSON, onsite task board, module completion document, and V1/V2 scope document, then reports requirement confirmation, P0 prototype/code completion, V1 go-live readiness, release gates, onsite role tasks, blocker groups, and planned V2 differences in Markdown and JSON. `package.json` exposes `npm run v1-completion-snapshot:check`, covering completion-percentage parsing, release gates, onsite roles, V2 differences, generated files, redaction, and missing release-candidate handling. The go-live handoff pack now auto-copies `v1-completion-snapshot.latest.md/json` when present. Running the snapshot against the current local state still reports `blocked`: requirements `85-90%`, P0 prototype/code `97-98%`, V1 go-live readiness `76-79%`, release candidate `0/4`, and onsite tasks `52`.

Latest V1 role-task distribution update: the onsite task-board generator now writes per-role Markdown files under `.erp-local-storage/v1-onsite-task-board/roles/*.latest.md` in addition to the combined `latest.md` / `latest.json` board. The go-live handoff pack now auto-copies those per-role files into `onsite-task-board-roles/*.latest.md` when present, and the handoff summary / manifest report how many role files were included. `npm run v1-onsite-task-board:check` and `npm run v1-go-live-handoff:check` now cover per-role file generation, handoff copying, file indexing, and redaction. This improves field assignment clarity, but it does not change the release conclusion: the current V1 state remains blocked until real production services, printer hardware, driver phones, pilot evidence, and signoff are completed.

Latest V1 handoff-pack task-board integration update: the go-live handoff pack now automatically includes the role-based onsite task board when `.erp-local-storage/v1-onsite-task-board/latest.md` / `latest.json` exist, or when explicit `--onsite-task-board-markdown` / `--onsite-task-board-json` paths are passed. The pack copies them as `v1-onsite-task-board.latest.md` and `v1-onsite-task-board.latest.json`, and the handoff summary / manifest now include task totals, role buckets, and redaction safeguards. `npm run v1-go-live-handoff:check` covers task-board inclusion, copied files, redaction, optional raw field evidence, required docs, and missing release-candidate handling. Running the handoff pack against the current local state still reports `blocked_handoff_written`, but the single handoff directory now contains the release candidate, redacted field evidence, onsite task board, env template, runbook, checklist, and V1/V2 scope snapshot.

Latest V1 onsite task-board update: `scripts/run-v1-onsite-task-board.mjs` now creates a role-based onsite task board under `.erp-local-storage/v1-onsite-task-board/`. It reads the latest release-candidate JSON plus a field-evidence manifest, then turns release blockers, missing evidence, missing signoffs, and V1/V2 boundary confirmation into tasks grouped for technical / management, office, warehouse / fulfillment, workshop, driver, and finance roles. The output writes Markdown and JSON `latest.*` files and stays redacted: it does not print raw `evidenceRef` values, signer names, real env values, command paths, or spool paths. `package.json` exposes `npm run v1-onsite-task-board:check`, covering role grouping, release blockers, evidence tasks, signoffs, boundary tasks, redaction, generated files, and missing release-candidate handling. Running it against the current local release candidate still reports `blocked` with 52 open tasks: 11 release-gate tasks, 34 evidence tasks, 6 signoff tasks, and 1 V1/V2 boundary task. This improves field execution clarity, but it does not make V1 complete; the current release remains blocked until real production services, printers, driver phones, pilot evidence, and signoff are completed.

Latest V1 go-live handoff pack update: `scripts/run-v1-go-live-handoff-pack.mjs` now creates a local V1 go-live handoff pack under `.erp-local-storage/v1-go-live-handoff/`. It reads the latest release-candidate JSON, the field-evidence manifest, the V1 production env template, the go-live runbook, the field-evidence checklist, and the V1/V2 scope document, then writes `handoff-summary.zh-CN.md`, `handoff-manifest.json`, release-candidate snapshots, a redacted field-evidence manifest snapshot, and the relevant V1 documents. The pack defaults to redaction: it does not copy real env files and does not print raw `evidenceRef` values; raw field evidence is copied only when `--include-raw-field-evidence` is explicit. `package.json` exposes `npm run v1-go-live-handoff:check`, covering blocked packs, redaction, optional raw evidence, required documents, and missing release-candidate handling. The current generated pack remains `blocked_handoff_written` with release candidate `0/4`; it improves field handoff clarity but does not make V1 complete without real production services, real printers, driver phones, pilot evidence, and signoff.

Latest V1 release-candidate field-evidence gate update: `scripts/run-v1-release-candidate-check.mjs` now includes the V1 field-evidence manifest as the fourth release-candidate gate. The runner accepts `--field-evidence-manifest <path>` or `ERP_V1_FIELD_EVIDENCE_MANIFEST`; when no filled manifest is provided it validates the checked-in template, which correctly remains blocked because every required evidence item defaults to `pending`. `scripts/check-v1-release-candidate-check.mjs` now requires a complete field-evidence manifest in the ready fixture and covers blocked, ready, env-file, archival blocked, field-evidence manifest, V1/V2 scope, report files, and redaction. The generated go-live runbook and field-evidence checklist now pass the filled manifest path into the release-candidate command. This makes the final go/no-go report stricter: V1 cannot be reported READY without production env preflight, field evidence, runtime readiness, and field-acceptance report all passing.

Latest V1 field-evidence manifest update: `scripts/generate-v1-field-evidence-manifest.mjs` now produces the checked-in V1 field-evidence JSON template and Chinese checklist at `docs/development/v1-field-evidence-manifest.template.json` and `docs/development/v1-field-evidence-checklist.zh-CN.md`. The manifest covers production persistence, object storage, real CUPS / printer hardware, driver native devices, real business workflow pilot evidence, security / operations controls, six required role signoffs, and explicit V1/V2 boundary confirmation. All required evidence items default to `pending` with empty `evidenceRef` values. `scripts/validate-v1-field-evidence-manifest.mjs` validates a filled manifest with `0 ready`, `1 invalid`, and `2 blocked` exit codes, and redacts evidence refs from reports so connection strings, secrets, command paths, and spool paths are not printed. `package.json` exposes `npm run v1-field-evidence:check`, and `scripts/check-v1-field-evidence-manifest.mjs` covers generator sync, default blocked state, a complete ready fixture, invalid missing-group schema failures, and redaction. This improves the onsite evidence handoff, but it does not change the current V1 conclusion: real production PostgreSQL / object storage, real CUPS / printers, real driver phones, business pilot evidence, and field signoff are still required before V1 can be called complete.

Latest V1 production environment template update: `scripts/generate-v1-production-env-template.mjs` now produces the redacted V1 production env template and go-live runbook used to move the current release candidate from `blocked` toward `ready`. The checked-in files are `docs/development/v1-production.env.example` and `docs/development/v1-go-live-runbook.zh-CN.md`; the env example keeps every value commented and uses placeholders only, covering the PostgreSQL profile, object storage, statement export storage, command bridge, CUPS preflight, readiness identities, field-acceptance output, release-candidate output, and local-bypass flags. `package.json` exposes `npm run v1-production-env-template:check`, and `scripts/check-v1-production-env-template.mjs` verifies generator sync, required variable coverage, runbook commands, V1/V2 boundary text, and absence of real secrets. This reduces deployment setup ambiguity but does not complete V1 until the template is filled with real production values and the release-candidate gate passes against real services and field evidence.

Latest V1 release-candidate check update: `scripts/run-v1-release-candidate-check.mjs` now combines the pre-start production environment preflight, the V1 field-evidence manifest gate, the running API V1 readiness runner, and the persisted field-acceptance report into one redacted release-candidate gate. It writes Markdown and JSON reports under `.erp-local-storage/v1-release-candidate/`, keeps `latest.*` pointers, includes explicit V1 scope and planned V2 differences, and exits with `0 ready`, `1 runner/API/write error`, or `2 blocked`. `package.json` exposes `npm run v1-release-candidate:check`, and `scripts/check-v1-release-candidate-check.mjs` covers blocked, ready, env-file, archival blocked, field-evidence manifest, V1/V2 scope, report files, and redaction. Running the release-candidate check against the current local target reports `0/4 release gates passed`, with production env preflight at `2/9 passed`, field evidence at `0/34 evidence items and 0/6 signoffs`, runtime readiness at `5/11 passed`, and field acceptance at `5/11 passed`. This improves the user-facing go/no-go view, but it does not change the current V1 conclusion: real production PostgreSQL / object storage, real CUPS / printers, real driver phones, and field signoff are still required before V1 can be called complete.

Latest V1 production environment preflight update: `scripts/run-v1-production-env-preflight.mjs` now provides a redacted pre-start configuration gate for real V1 deployment. It checks whether the environment explicitly selects the PostgreSQL V1 persistence profile, object-storage file profile, attachment / statement export object-storage credentials, system-printer command bridge, CUPS non-printing preflight settings, readiness identities, field-acceptance report output, and local-bypass flags. The runner is non-mutating, supports `--env-file`, emits text or JSON, and exits with `0 ready`, `1 runner/env-file error`, or `2 blocked`. `package.json` exposes `npm run v1-production-env-preflight:check`, and `scripts/check-v1-production-env-preflight.mjs` covers empty blocked env, complete ready env, env-file parsing, invalid JSON blockers, warning-only local bypass detection, exit codes, and redaction of database URLs, object-storage values, command paths, and spool paths. Running the preflight against the current local shell reports `2/9 passed` and `blocked`, which is expected because the current local instance is still not configured as a real production V1 environment.

Latest V1 field-acceptance report update: `scripts/run-v1-field-acceptance-report.mjs` now turns the existing top-level V1 readiness runner into a persisted field-acceptance package. It writes redacted Markdown and JSON reports under `.erp-local-storage/v1-field-acceptance/`, including the current `11` readiness criteria, module-level evidence state, blocking criteria, required onsite evidence, safeguards, and next actions. `package.json` now exposes `npm run v1-field-acceptance-report:check`, and `scripts/check-v1-field-acceptance-report.mjs` covers blocked reports, ready reports, archival blocked reports, generated `latest.*` files, exit codes, and redaction. A report generated against the current local API remains `blocked` at `5/11 passed`, which is the intended result for the default local runtime. Remaining boundary: this improves handoff and auditability, but it does not replace real production PostgreSQL / object-storage credentials, real CUPS / printer hardware QA, real driver phones, or onsite signoff.

Latest V1 production profile live check update: `scripts/check-v1-production-profile-live.mjs` now verifies the production runtime shape in two phases. Phase 1 starts temporary `postgres:16-alpine`, applies all migrations, starts a local S3-compatible object-storage endpoint, then starts the ERP API with `v1PersistenceProfile.repositoryMode=postgres` and `fileStorageMode=object_storage`; system persistence and attachment retention pass, and the top-level runner correctly reports `7/11 passed` with only print spool, CUPS, print V1, and driver native-device gates blocked. Phase 2 restarts the same production-profile API with a non-printing command-bridge / fake-CUPS configuration, inserts a minimal PostgreSQL delivery task, writes printer-device QA evidence and driver native-device QA evidence through the API, then proves the top-level runner can reach `11/11 passed` while keeping non-printing and redaction safeguards. The check also verifies that the QA evidence is persisted in PostgreSQL. Earlier in this work, `server/attachmentRepository.mjs` was fixed to preserve safe non-default object-storage key prefixes instead of dropping any key that did not start with `attachments/`. Validation passed: `node --check scripts/check-v1-production-profile-live.mjs` and `npm run v1-production-profile-live:check`. Remaining boundary: the `11/11` result is automated lab evidence, not real go-live approval; real PostgreSQL credentials, real OSS/S3/COS bucket, production network/policy/backup controls, real CUPS / printer hardware QA, real driver phones, and field acceptance remain open.

Latest attachment object-storage live check update: the attachment storage path now has an automated HTTP-level S3-compatible live compatibility check. New `scripts/check-attachment-object-storage-live.mjs` starts a local S3-compatible HTTP endpoint, starts the ERP API in `object_storage` mode, then verifies business attachment upload, permission-based content readback, object-storage signed URL generation and direct fetch, access audit logging, `/api/attachments/storage-diagnostics`, `/api/attachments/v1-readiness`, diagnostic-object cleanup, SigV4 header presence, and redaction of secret material. `package.json` now exposes this as `npm run attachment-storage-live:check`, and it passed together with `node --check scripts/check-attachment-object-storage-live.mjs`. Remaining boundary: this proves the ERP code can operate against an HTTP object-storage endpoint, but it is still not a real production OSS/S3/COS bucket rollout; production credentials, network policy, lifecycle, backup monitoring, virus scanning, resumable upload, and field acceptance remain open.

Latest master-data import review persistence update: `masterDataImportReviewRepository` now has a PostgreSQL mode instead of being local-JSON only. `server/masterDataImportReviewRepository.mjs` can persist review drafts, confirmation plans, import executions, and related operation logs through PostgreSQL transactions; `db/migrations/0007_master_data_import_review.sql` adds `master_data_import_review_drafts`, `master_data_import_confirmation_plans`, and `master_data_import_executions` with common indexed fields plus JSONB payloads. The unified V1 persistence profile now covers `masterDataImportReviewRepositoryOptions`, so `unsupportedRepositoryCount` is 0 and the profile applies PostgreSQL defaults to 26 supported repositories. Validation passed: `node --check` for touched MJS files, `npm run master-data-review-repository:check`, `npm run db:check`, `npm run db:migrate:dry`, `npm run v1-persistence-profile:check`, and `npm run db:postgres-live:check`; the live check used a temporary `postgres:16-alpine` database to prove migration, write, read, and API startup through the unified profile. Remaining boundary: the current local runtime still starts in local mode unless deployed with real PostgreSQL / object-storage settings, and V1 remains blocked by object storage, print field QA, driver native-device QA, and production deployment evidence.

Latest system V1 persistence readiness update: the project now has a dedicated go-live gate for production persistence instead of relying on `/api/health` repository-kind visibility. `GET /api/system/v1-readiness` is read-only and groups 28 core repositories across order / inventory / fulfillment, statements / payments, production / driver, evidence / audit / print / master data, and file-retention stores. By default, `local_memory`, `local_json`, and `local_fs` block V1 production readiness unless local persistence is explicitly accepted through server configuration. The response exposes only repository kinds and counts, not business payloads, connection strings, local paths, or secrets. `scripts/run-v1-readiness-check.mjs` now has 11 top-level criteria and includes `system-v1-persistence`; the live local API currently reports `5/11 passed`, with system persistence at `1/7 passed` and 28 local repositories, 19 of them `local_memory`. Validation passed: `node --check` for touched files, `npm run api:validate-openapi`, `npm run api:check`, `npm run v1-readiness:check`, `npm run attachment-storage:check`, `npm run attachment-api:check`, `npm run build`, and `git diff --check`. Remaining boundary: the prototype is runnable, but V1 is not complete until the core repositories move to PostgreSQL / object storage, or the business explicitly accepts local persistence risk with backup, concurrency, disk, permission, and disaster-recovery controls.

Latest attachment V1 retention readiness update: attachment storage now has a separate V1 go-live gate instead of relying only on the lower-level read/write diagnostic. `GET /api/attachments/v1-readiness`, guarded by `attachment.view`, is read-only and combines the existing storage diagnostic, diagnostic-object cleanup, configuration completeness, production-retention storage mode, and secret redaction safeguards. The default local filesystem adapter can still pass `/attachments/storage-diagnostics`, but it now remains blocked for V1 retention unless object storage is live or local filesystem retention is explicitly accepted through server configuration / `ERP_ATTACHMENT_LOCAL_FS_V1_ACCEPTED`. `scripts/run-v1-readiness-check.mjs` now includes `attachment-v1-readiness` as the tenth top-level criterion; the automated ready fixture uses explicit local-retention acceptance so the test does not pretend a normal local folder is production object storage. Validation passed: `node --check server/apiServer.mjs`, `node --check scripts/run-v1-readiness-check.mjs`, `node --check scripts/check-v1-readiness-runner.mjs`, `node --check scripts/check-api-skeleton.mjs`, `npm run api:validate-openapi`, `npm run api:check`, `npm run attachment-storage:check`, and `npm run v1-readiness:check`. Remaining boundary: this prevents false-positive attachment readiness, but real OSS/S3/COS bucket rollout, credential rotation, network policy, lifecycle rules, virus scanning, resumable upload, backup monitoring, and field acceptance remain unfinished.

Latest driver V1 readiness update: driver mobile now has an aggregated V1 native-device go-live gate, and the top-level ERP V1 readiness CLI includes it. `GET /api/driver/v1-readiness`, guarded by `delivery.view`, is read-only and combines driver delivery task readback, the latest driver device field-test record, all six field checks, native package-scan bridge availability, native navigation bridge availability, and a matched physical package-label scan sample. Browser-only `native 0/2` evidence remains blocked, and a positive package-label sample must use the native SDK path. `scripts/run-v1-readiness-check.mjs` now supports a separate driver validation identity through `--driver-operator-id` / `ERP_V1_READINESS_DRIVER_OPERATOR_ID`; by default the office user checks attachment / print readiness, while the driver user checks driver readiness, so the runner does not require expanding office permissions. Validation passed: `node --check server/apiServer.mjs`, `node --check scripts/run-v1-readiness-check.mjs`, `node --check scripts/check-v1-readiness-runner.mjs`, `node --check scripts/check-api-skeleton.mjs`, `npm run api:validate-openapi`, `npm run api:check`, and `npm run v1-readiness:check`. Remaining boundary: the automated ready fixture uses simulated native bridge diagnostics and sample payloads; real Android / iOS shells, phone permissions, physical package labels, map-app opening, watermarked delivery photos, production deployment, and on-site driver QA remain unfinished.

Latest live CUPS queue preflight runner update: a direct non-printing CUPS queue preflight CLI is now available for field machines. `scripts/run-cups-queue-preflight.mjs` wraps the existing `scripts/print-command-bridge.mjs --action cups-preflight` path, defaults to an `lpstat -p {cupsPrinterName}` style status command, supports printer / allowlist / status-command / status-args / timeout / JSON options, and exits with `0` for ready, `1` for runner or bridge errors, and `2` for a runnable but blocked preflight. The runner does not read order payload, create print files, submit print jobs, or expose command paths, stdout/stderr contents, spool paths, or payload details. `scripts/check-cups-queue-preflight-runner.mjs` and `npm run cups-queue-preflight:check` cover ready, missing allowlist, failing queue, redaction, and exit codes using the fake CUPS status command. Validation passed: `node --check scripts/run-cups-queue-preflight.mjs`, `node --check scripts/check-cups-queue-preflight-runner.mjs`, and `npm run cups-queue-preflight:check`. Remaining boundary: automated checks still use a fake status command; a real field machine must run this against the actual `lpstat`, real queue names, and printer allowlist before the project can claim real CUPS queue reachability. Physical label-printer / dot-matrix output, paper alignment, barcode scanning, vendor SDKs, physical status protocols, and on-site QA remain unfinished.

Latest print V1 readiness runner update: a reusable local / on-site command-line runner now checks the running ERP API's print readiness boundary. `scripts/run-print-v1-readiness-check.mjs` reads `GET /api/print-driver/cups-diagnostics` and `GET /api/print-driver/v1-readiness`, supports text or `--json` output, accepts API base URL / operator / bearer-token options, redacts command paths, spool paths, stdout/stderr contents, and payload details, and exits with `0` for ready, `1` for API/read errors, and `2` for a reachable but blocked gate. `scripts/check-print-v1-readiness-runner.mjs` and `npm run print-v1-readiness:check` cover the default blocked gate, a fully prepared ready gate, CUPS preflight, redaction, and exit codes. During validation, the first check hung because the test started the API server and then used `spawnSync` for the runner in the same Node process, blocking the event loop so the child could not receive API responses; the check now uses async `spawn`, request timeouts, connection-close headers, server-close fallback, and explicit spool directory setup. Validation passed: `node --check scripts/run-print-v1-readiness-check.mjs`, `node --check scripts/check-print-v1-readiness-runner.mjs`, and `npm run print-v1-readiness:check`. Remaining boundary: this standardizes readiness verification but still does not call the host's real `lpstat`, prove physical label-printer / dot-matrix output, paper alignment, barcode scanning, vendor SDKs, physical status protocols, or on-site hardware QA.

Latest CUPS readiness UI/gate update: CUPS queue diagnostics are now visible in the office print diagnostics panel and part of the V1 print go-live gate. `src/services/officePrintDriverConfigApiClient.js` now has `getOfficePrintDriverCupsDiagnostics(...)` plus client-side normalization, `src/App.jsx` refreshes CUPS diagnostics when entering `打包/标签` and in the page refresh bundle, and `src/pages/office/index.jsx` shows a `CUPS 队列预检` section with printer-name, allowlist, status-command, and non-printing safeguard rows. `GET /api/print-driver/v1-readiness` now returns `cupsDiagnostics` and includes `cups-queue-preflight` as the ninth blocking criterion. Validation passed: touched-file `node --check`, `npm run print-driver-config-api:check`, `npm run api:validate-openapi`, `npm run api:check`, and `npm run build`. Browser verification used a temporary `5175 + 8791` stack with the fake CUPS status command and confirmed `CUPS 队列预检`, `队列可访问`, and `5/9 通过` at `1280x720` and `390x844`, with zero ERP page errors and no horizontal overflow; screenshots: `screenshots/p0-print-driver-cups-diagnostics-1280.png` and `screenshots/p0-print-driver-cups-diagnostics-390.png`. Remaining boundary: this still uses `scripts/fake-cups-lpstat.mjs`, not the host's real CUPS queue, and does not prove physical label-printer / dot-matrix output, paper alignment, barcode scanning, vendor SDKs, physical status protocols, or on-site hardware QA.

Latest CUPS queue preflight update: the print integration now has a first non-printing CUPS queue diagnostics boundary. `scripts/print-command-bridge.mjs` supports `--action cups-preflight`, which runs a CUPS status command such as `lpstat -p {cupsPrinterName}` without reading job payload from stdin, creating a print file, or submitting a physical print job. The action still requires `cups_lp` mode, a CUPS printer name, and a CUPS printer allowlist, and returns only redacted readiness fields such as `ready`, `cupsPrinterAllowed`, `cupsStatusCommandRunnable`, `stdoutBytes`, `stderrBytes`, and `errorCode`. `server/printDriverAdapter.mjs` now exposes `runCupsDiagnostics(...)`, and `GET /api/print-driver/cups-diagnostics` is guarded by `fulfillment.print`; finance users are denied. OpenAPI, the print integration guide, and module completion status are updated. Validation passed: touched-file `node --check`, `npm run print-command-bridge:check`, `npm run print-driver-adapter:check`, `npm run api:validate-openapi`, and `npm run api:check`. Remaining boundary: automated checks use `scripts/fake-cups-lpstat.mjs`, not the host's real CUPS queue; real label-printer / dot-matrix output, paper alignment, barcode scanning, vendor SDKs, physical status protocols, and on-site hardware QA are still not proven.

Latest CUPS bridge boundary update: the local print command bridge now supports an optional `cups_lp` mode in addition to the default safe `spool_only` mode. `scripts/print-command-bridge.mjs` still does not touch a physical printer by default. When explicitly configured with `--mode cups_lp` / `ERP_PRINT_COMMAND_BRIDGE_MODE=cups_lp`, a CUPS command, a CUPS printer allowlist, and a printer name, the bridge writes the local job record, generates a controlled plain-text print file, and calls `lp` or the configured CUPS command with `shell:false`. A successful CUPS acceptance moves the bridge record to `sent` and returns only redacted identifiers such as `externalJobId`, `bridgeJobId`, `status`, `mode`, and `cupsJobId`; the ERP print job remains `sent` until spool completion, driver callback, or field confirmation marks it printed. The office print-driver integration kit now documents both `spool_only` and `cups_lp`. Validation passed: `node --check scripts/print-command-bridge.mjs`, `node --check scripts/fake-cups-lp.mjs`, `node --check scripts/check-print-command-bridge.mjs`, and `npm run print-command-bridge:check`. Remaining boundary: this adds the code boundary for CUPS submission and uses a fake lp command in automated checks; real label-printer / dot-matrix output, paper alignment, barcode scanning, vendor SDKs, physical status protocols, and on-site hardware QA are still not proven.

Latest print-device field-QA evidence update: printer device field QA now records a structured evidence summary instead of relying only on six manual check statuses and a free-text note. The office `打包/标签` print-device QA panel now shows `验收证据` inputs for sample / paper evidence, barcode scan evidence, driver or spool callback evidence, void / reprint evidence, and on-site operator acceptance. `recordOfficePrinterDeviceFieldTest(...)`, `POST /api/print-devices/{printDeviceId}/field-tests`, local JSON / PostgreSQL repository paths, and OpenAPI now persist and return `evidence` plus `evidenceSummary`. The V1 print go-live gate now requires both all six field-QA checks to pass and the evidence summary to be complete before a device can be considered ready; the positive API skeleton readiness fixture now supplies complete evidence. Validation passed: `node --check` for touched JS/MJS files, `npm run printer-device-field-test:check`, `npm run printer-device-api:check`, `npm run api:validate-openapi`, `npm run api:check`, and `npm run build`. Browser verification on `5174` confirmed the evidence section and all five evidence labels are visible at `1280x720` and `390x844`, with zero ERP page errors and no horizontal overflow; screenshots: `screenshots/p0-printer-device-qa-evidence-1280.png` and `screenshots/p0-printer-device-qa-evidence-390.png`. Remaining boundary: this makes field-QA evidence recordable and gateable, but does not complete real CUPS / label-printer / dot-matrix output, barcode scanning, vendor SDK integration, physical printer status protocols, or on-site hardware acceptance.

Latest print device mode update: the office `打包/标签` print-device QA panel now includes a narrow device-mode maintenance flow. Office users can see the current mode, choose `preview_only` or `system_printer`, and click `保存设备模式`; the frontend calls `POST /api/print-devices/{printDeviceId}/driver-mode` through `updateOfficePrintDeviceDriverMode(...)`, uses the existing `fulfillment.print` permission, and refreshes the V1 print go-live readiness gate after a successful save. The dedicated route only updates `settings.driverMode`, `updatedBy`, and `updatedAt`, so a mode toggle cannot accidentally overwrite paper size, DPI, supported document types, or other device profile fields. Mode changes are deliberately separate from field QA records, and API/network failure blocks the save instead of creating a local fake success. Browser verification on the current `5174 + 8787` stack changed `标签机A` from `preview_only` to `system_printer`, read back `settings.driverMode=system_printer` from the API, and confirmed readiness moved to `3/8 passed, 5 blockers`; remaining blockers are system-printer configuration, spool readback, label-printer field QA, dot-matrix `preview_only`, and dot-matrix field QA. Validation passed: `node --check server/apiServer.mjs`, `node --check src/services/officePrinterDeviceApiClient.js`, `node --check scripts/check-api-skeleton.mjs`, `node --check scripts/check-frontend-printer-device-api-client.mjs`, `npm run printer-device-api:check`, `npm run api:validate-openapi`, `npm run api:check`, `npm run auth:check`, `npm run seed:check`, `npm run print-driver-config-api:check`, `npm run build`, and `git diff --check`. Screenshots: `screenshots/p0-printer-device-mode-office-1280.png` and `screenshots/p0-printer-device-mode-office-390.png`. Remaining boundary: this lets office maintain ERP device mode but does not prove real CUPS / label-printer / dot-matrix output, paper alignment, barcode scanning, vendor SDKs, physical status protocols, or on-site operator acceptance.

Latest print readiness UI update: the office `打包/标签` page now surfaces the V1 print go-live gate instead of leaving it as an API-only check. The frontend API client reads `GET /api/print-driver/v1-readiness`, and the detail pane now shows `V1 打印上线门禁` with gate status, blocker count, required document coverage, required device groups, all eight readiness criteria, label-printer / dot-matrix summaries, remaining V1 risks, and non-printing safeguards. Browser verification on the current `5174 + 8787` stack switched from a workshop account, which was correctly denied by `fulfillment.print`, to office user A and confirmed the default environment shows `2/8 passed, 6 blockers`, both devices still in `preview_only`, no ERP page errors, and no page-level horizontal overflow at `1280x720` or `390x844`. Remaining boundary: this makes readiness visible to office users, but does not complete real CUPS / label-printer / dot-matrix output, paper alignment, barcode scanning, vendor SDKs, physical status protocols, or on-site operator acceptance.

Latest print V1 readiness gate update: the print integration now has a first aggregated V1 go-live readiness report. `GET /api/print-driver/v1-readiness`, guarded by `fulfillment.print`, combines system-printer configuration, environment preflight, non-printing spool readback diagnostics, required V1 print devices, device driver mode, and latest field QA records. The default local environment correctly reports `blocked / ready=false` because system printing is disabled, spool readback is not configured, default devices are still `preview_only`, and no field QA has passed for the label and dot-matrix devices. The API skeleton now also starts a temporary positive-readiness server, configures command_bridge, switches both required devices to `system_printer`, records all six QA checks as passed, and verifies the report can return `ready=true` without exposing command paths or spool paths. Validation passed: `node --check server/apiServer.mjs`, `node --check scripts/check-api-skeleton.mjs`, `npm run api:validate-openapi`, and `npm run api:check`. Remaining boundary: this is a go-live gate over configuration and recorded QA evidence, not a substitute for real CUPS / label-printer / dot-matrix output, paper alignment, barcode scanning, vendor SDKs, physical status protocols, or on-site operator acceptance.

Latest print command-bridge spool diagnostics update: the print driver now has a first V1 non-printing runtime diagnostic for command-bridge status readback. `GET /api/print-driver/spool-diagnostics`, guarded by `fulfillment.print`, writes a diagnostic spool record, verifies `queued` is read back as pending / sent, verifies `completed` is read back as printed, and cleans up the diagnostic files. The route does not create a business print job, does not invoke the configured print command, and keeps command paths, command args, spool paths, and print payloads redacted. Validation passed: touched-file `node --check`, `npm run print-driver-adapter:check`, `npm run api:validate-openapi`, and `npm run api:check`. Remaining boundary: this proves ERP-side spool file write / readback / cleanup behavior, not CUPS, label-printer, dot-matrix, vendor SDK, paper alignment, barcode scan, physical printer status protocol, or field print QA acceptance.

Latest attachment storage diagnostics update: attachment object storage now has a first V1 runtime preflight route. `GET /api/attachments/storage-diagnostics`, guarded by `attachment.view`, writes a small diagnostic object, reads it back, verifies the sha256 digest, and attempts cleanup without registering a business attachment. The response reports storage mode, configuration completeness, missing configuration fields, write / read / digest / cleanup checks, and keeps `secretFieldsExposed=false`; users without attachment-view permission are denied. `server/attachmentObjectStorage.mjs` now supports `deleteObject(...)` for local filesystem and S3-compatible adapters so preflight does not leave diagnostic objects behind. Validation passed: touched-file `node --check`, `npm run attachment-storage:check`, `npm run api:validate-openapi`, and `npm run api:check`. Remaining boundary: this is a runtime adapter preflight, not a completed production OSS/S3/COS bucket rollout, credential lifecycle, network policy, virus scanning, resumable upload, or field acceptance of attachment workflows.

Latest attachment upload validation update: attachment creation now has a first V1 purpose-specific safety gate on both the frontend API client and the backend route. `createOfficeAttachment(...)` validates type, MIME, and size before sending the request, while `POST /api/attachments` repeats the same enforcement server-side and re-infers `fileType` from MIME / filename. Current rules are: payment screenshots must be images up to 8 MB; delivery watermark photos, signature photos, and custom printed finished-goods photos must be images up to 12 MB; customer-confirmation attachments may be image or PDF up to 12 MB; other attachments default to image / PDF / spreadsheet / document up to 15 MB. The existing local / S3-compatible attachment object-storage boundary remains the content-retention layer. Validation passed: touched-file `node --check`, `npm run attachment-api:check`, `npm run api:check`, `npm run workshop-mobile-api:check`, `npm run statement-api:check`, `npm run driver-mobile-api:check`, and `npm run attachment-storage:check`. Remaining boundary: this is not image-quality automation, virus scanning, resumable upload, a real OSS/S3/COS bucket live proof, or field-tested phone-camera acceptance.

Latest workshop finished-goods photo update: workshop mobile now has a first real photo-upload entry for custom printed finished goods, guarded by a narrow attachment permission. The `车间/打包手机端` production detail shows a `定制成品图` section with current attachment, uploaded time, office review time, rejection reason, an `image/*` file input with `capture=environment`, and an `上传成品图 / 重拍/重传成品图` action. The action reuses the existing attachment API plus `POST /api/production-tasks/{productionTaskId}/finished-goods-photo`; a selected browser/mobile file is uploaded as a data URL, and the prototype sample file is only a fallback when no file is selected. Uploading from workshop sets the photo to `待确认` and does not create a customer-notification todo until office review. The backend now has a dedicated `attachment.finished_goods_photo.create` permission: workshop users have it, but still do not have general `attachment.create`, so they can create production-task finished-goods-photo attachments only, not payment screenshots or statement confirmation attachments. Validation passed: `npm run seed:check`, `npm run workshop-mobile-api:check`, `npm run production-packing-api:check`, `npm run auth:check`, `npm run api:check`, `npm run build`, and `git diff --check`; the build still only has the existing Vite chunk warning. Browser verification on current `5174 + 8787` switched to `车间A`, confirmed the photo input and upload button, clicked upload, then read back `finishedGoodsPhoto.status=待确认`, `uploadedBy=U-WORKSHOP-A`, `reviewedAt=""`, and zero open `待通知客户` todos. Both `1280x720` and `390x844` viewports had no horizontal overflow and zero ERP page errors. Remaining boundary: this still has not been field-tested on a real phone camera permission prompt or real workshop photo-quality acceptance.

Latest customer-notification todo update: `待通知客户` public todos now have a first actionable V1 loop instead of only being generated by finished-goods-photo review. The public todo page now reads `GET /api/todos?status=all` when the backend is available, so API-created customer-notification todos are visible in the office shared todo pool. Selecting one shows a `客户通知` detail section with manual-send channel, notification status, photo prompt, and copyable customer message text. New todo actions `customer_notification_copied` and `customer_notification_sent` keep the business boundary explicit: copying the message records `话术已复制` and leaves the todo open, while confirming manual customer notification closes the todo and records `已通知客户`. The frontend actions `复制通知话术` and `确认已通知客户`, the API client, OpenAPI draft, API skeleton check, and local fallback state helpers are updated. Validation passed: `npm run api:check`, `npm run todo-api:check`, `npm run build`, and `git diff --check`; the build still only has the existing Vite 500 kB chunk warning. Browser verification on the current `5174 + 8787` stack confirmed `后端公共待办 9 条`, `待通知客户`, `客户通知`, `成品图发您确认`, and both action buttons are visible, then confirmed the todo and read back `status=handled` with `notificationStatus=已通知客户`. A direct API check also verified that copy keeps the todo open and send confirmation closes it. Remaining boundary: V1 still means manual copy / manual send / manual confirmation, not WeCom auto-send, customer-group callbacks, automatic read receipts, automatic customer confirmation, or real workshop-phone photo capture.

Latest finished-goods photo quality-flow update: custom printed production tasks now have a first upload / office-review / customer-notification-todo loop. The office `打包/标签` production-task detail shows a `定制成品图` section; uploading a finished-goods photo sets the task photo status to `待确认`, accepting it sets `已接受` and creates or deduplicates a `待通知客户` public todo, and rejecting it creates or deduplicates a `成品图需重拍` todo. New API routes are `POST /api/production-tasks/{productionTaskId}/finished-goods-photo` and `/finished-goods-photo-review`, guarded by `production.report.complete` and `production.schedule.publish`. The flow intentionally creates no inventory, reservations, packing tasks, or automatic customer messages; customer notification remains an office manual action. Attachments use `ownerType=production_task` and `purpose=finished_goods_photo`, production task list/detail read models now return `finishedGoodsPhoto`, and operation logs record upload / accept / reject actions. Validation passed: touched-file `node --check`, `npm run production-packing-api:check`, `npm run api:check`, `npm run auth:check`, `npm run api:validate-openapi`, `npm run build`, and `git diff --check`; the build still only has the existing Vite 500 kB chunk warning. Browser verification on the current `5174 + 8787` stack uploaded and accepted the sample photo for `PT-ORD-0629-003-01`, changed the public-todo badge from 8 to 9, read back `status=已接受`, `required=true`, and `attachmentId=ATT-010`, saw zero ERP page errors, and confirmed no horizontal overflow at `1280x720` or `390x844`. Screenshots: `screenshots/p0-production-finished-photo-1280.png` and `screenshots/p0-production-finished-photo-390.png`. Remaining boundary: this is still a V1 local sample / manual review / manual customer-notification-todo loop, not real workshop phone capture, automatic customer sending, object-storage live proof, image-quality automation, or field acceptance.

Latest scheduling move audit update: office machine-queue move / insert now includes an explicit reason selector, live impact preview, and audit-friendly remark text. In `打包/标签`, the queue move controls now include reasons such as `主管安排`, `急单插入`, `交期风险`, `等料调整`, `机器问题`, and `机台平衡`; the UI previews how many source-machine rows will be resequenced and how many target-machine rows will be pushed down before the user clicks `移动/插队`. Successful moves include the reason in the toast and persist the reason plus impact summary in `sequenceRemark`; queue status now surfaces record-source labels such as manual resequence, machine reassignment, and manual insertion. Validation passed: `npm run production-packing-api:check`, `npm run api:check`, `npm run api:validate-openapi`, `npm run build`, and `git diff --check`; direct `node --check` on `.jsx` files is not supported by this Node runtime and returned `ERR_UNKNOWN_FILE_EXTENSION`, so JSX syntax is covered by the Vite build. Browser verification on the current `5174 + 8787` stack selected `急单插入`, moved `PT-ORD-0629-003-01` from `BAG-01 #1` to `BAG-02 #1`, read back `sequenceRemark` with the urgent reason and impact summary, saw zero ERP page errors, and confirmed no horizontal overflow at `1280x720` or `390x844`. Screenshots: `screenshots/p0-production-queue-move-impact-1280.png` and `screenshots/p0-production-queue-move-impact-390.png`. Latest machine-queue UI completion update: office scheduling now has a visible cross-machine move / insert loop in the `打包/标签` machine queue. The queue header now shows compact target-machine, target-position, and `移动/插队` controls; target machines include current queue machines, production-task machines, and P0 fallback machines so an office user can move a task onto an empty machine. The action is mapped to the existing `production.schedule.sequence.update` permission and calls `moveOfficeProductionMachineQueueItem(...)` / `POST /api/production-schedules/machine-queue/move`; after success the frontend refreshes machine-queue state and the local production-task machine id, while the toast still states that the action creates no inventory, reservation, or packing task. Validation passed: `node --check src/services/officeProductionPackingApiClient.js`, `node --check scripts/check-frontend-production-packing-api-client.mjs`, `node --check src/auth/seedPermissions.js`, `npm run production-packing-api:check`, `npm run auth:check`, `npm run api:check`, `npm run build`, and `git diff --check`; the build still only has the existing Vite 500 kB chunk warning. Latest machine-move scheduling update: office scheduling now has a first cross-machine move / insert API boundary for the machine queue. New `POST /api/production-schedules/machine-queue/move`, guarded by `production.schedule.sequence.update`, moves a production task to a target machine and target queue position, updates `production_tasks.machine_id`, marks the source machine schedule record as `moved`, writes the target machine record as `active`, and records `machine_reassignment` plus an operation log. The frontend production / packing API client now exposes `moveOfficeProductionMachineQueueItem(...)`; OpenAPI, API skeleton checks, frontend API-client checks, repository SQL checks, and PostgreSQL live checks are updated. `ProductionScheduleRecordSummary.queueSeq` now allows `0` for moved source-history records that are no longer executable queue positions. Browser verification on `打包/标签` confirmed `机台排产队列`, `BAG-02 #1`, and `PT-ORD-0629-003-01` are visible, ERP page errors are zero, and the current desktop width has no horizontal overflow. Latest formal schedule-record boundary update: same-machine machine-queue resequencing now has a first formal record repository and PostgreSQL table boundary instead of only local skeleton state. Latest machine-queue scheduling update: office scheduling now has a first derived machine queue visible in the production / packing workspace. Latest statement XLSX export update: statement exports now produce real `.xlsx` Open XML binary workbooks instead of SpreadsheetML `.xls` XML files. `src/domain/statementExcelTemplate.js` generates a ZIP-based workbook with `对账汇总` and `交付明细` sheets, compact factory goods specs, money styles, confirmation fields, internal review fields, and `templateVersion=p0-statement-xlsx-v1`; the legacy SpreadsheetML builder remains only as a compatibility helper. `server/apiServer.mjs`, `server/statementExportObjectStorage.mjs`, `server/statementExportRepository.mjs`, `src/services/officeStatementApiClient.js`, and `src/App.jsx` now handle export content as binary / base64, return `.xlsx` filenames, and use the OpenXML MIME type. Latest statement export object-storage retention update: statement export files now get a real file-storage boundary before repository persistence. `server/statementExportObjectStorage.mjs` reuses the existing local/S3-compatible object-storage implementation for statement workbooks; default exports are written to `.erp-local-storage/statement-exports/<downloadToken>/...`, while `ERP_STATEMENT_EXPORT_OBJECT_STORAGE=object_storage` can use S3-compatible PUT/GET signing with statement-specific bucket/key-prefix environment variables. `server/apiServer.mjs` now writes each generated statement workbook to this storage layer before persisting `statement_export_files`, keeps `content_text` as a compatibility fallback, and downloads historical exports by reading storage first and falling back to database content only if needed. Latest command-bridge lifecycle update: `scripts/print-command-bridge.mjs` now supports explicit local spool lifecycle actions in addition to the existing default submit behavior. `--action status` returns a sanitized lifecycle summary, `--action complete` moves a queued bridge job to `completed`, `--action fail` moves it to `failed` with a sanitized error code/message, and `--action cancel` moves it to `canceled` with a sanitized reason; terminal jobs cannot be moved to a different terminal state. The script still writes only inside the configured local storage / spool root and does not expose payload contents in lifecycle responses. `scripts/check-print-command-bridge.mjs` now covers status, complete, fail, cancel, terminal lock, and no payload leakage; `scripts/check-print-job-command-bridge-status-readback.mjs` now uses the bridge `complete` action to produce completed spool evidence before API polling maps the print job to `printed`. Latest fulfillment note template update: self-pickup and delivery now have first-version dot-matrix document templates instead of sharing the express / LTL label path. `src/domain/printTemplates.js` now generates `pickup_note` / `delivery_note` templates with 241mm continuous two-copy paper, line-item rows, quantity / package text, price snapshot display, total amount, copy labels, receiver signature label, and method-specific titles. Express / LTL labels still hide money fields. `server/apiServer.mjs` now derives the print document type from the fulfillment method so pickup / delivery cannot be routed to the express label printer by mistake; `PRN-DOT-A` now advertises `outbound_note`, `pickup_note`, and `delivery_note` as default document types. The print preview UI shows the line table and price section only when `priceHidden=false`. Latest print command wrapper update: the first local print command wrapper now exists as `scripts/print-command-bridge.mjs`. It is intentionally `spool_only` by default: the adapter can execute it through `command_bridge`, the wrapper validates stdin JSON plus job / device identifiers, writes queued bridge records to `.erp-local-storage/print-command-bridge/queued/*.json`, and returns a sanitized `externalJobId` that the API records as `command_sent` / `sent`. This gives deployment a concrete command target for local bridge testing without claiming physical print success. New validation `npm run print-command-bridge:check` covers direct wrapper success, identifier mismatch, invalid JSON, spool-path confinement, adapter integration, and response redaction. Validation passed: touched-file `node --check`, `npm run print-command-bridge:check`, `npm run print-driver-adapter:check`, `npm run api:validate-openapi`, `npm run api:check`, `npm run db:check`, `npm run db:migrate:dry`, `npm run db:postgres-live:check`, `npm run build`, and `git diff --check`. Remaining boundary: a real CUPS / label-printer / dot-matrix execution mode, physical QA, printer-status readback, vendor SDK bridges, and self-pickup / delivery dot-matrix templates remain unwired.

Latest print command-bridge update: the system-printer command bridge now has a controlled execution boundary instead of only reporting "not implemented." `server/printDriverAdapter.mjs` can submit a `system_printer` job through `command_bridge` when system printing is explicitly enabled, a command is configured, and the target device matches `ERP_SYSTEM_PRINTER_ALLOWLIST`. The bridge uses `spawnSync` without shell execution, renders a small argument template, sends a sanitized JSON job payload on stdin, and stores only sanitized diagnostics in dispatch metadata. A successful command exit marks the print job `sent` with `adapterStatus=command_sent`; it does not mark the job `printed`, because physical completion still requires a driver callback, future status readback, or operator confirmation. Failed commands return `SYSTEM_PRINTER_COMMAND_FAILED` / timeout / exception codes without leaking the command path, args, stdout, or stderr. OpenAPI remains at 53 paths / 142 schemas with `command_sent` and sanitized dispatch metadata documented. Validation passed: touched-file `node --check`, `npm run print-driver-adapter:check`, `npm run print-job-status-polling:check`, `npm run print-job-driver-status:check`, `npm run print-job-repository:check`, `npm run api:validate-openapi`, and `npm run api:check`. Remaining boundary: a production command wrapper, physical CUPS / label-printer / dot-matrix QA, printer-status readback, vendor SDK bridges, and self-pickup / delivery dot-matrix templates remain unwired.

Latest print driver configuration boundary update: system-printer integration now has a diagnosable configuration guard before any real OS or vendor bridge is implemented. `server/printDriverAdapter.mjs` now exposes `getConfiguration()` and reads guarded options for `ERP_SYSTEM_PRINTER_ADAPTER`, `ERP_SYSTEM_PRINTER_COMMAND`, and `ERP_SYSTEM_PRINTER_ALLOWLIST`; command values are never exposed in responses. If system printing is enabled without a concrete bridge, without a command, or for a device outside the allowlist, dispatch and status polling now fail with explicit error codes instead of falling through to a vague adapter-missing state. `server/apiServer.mjs` exposes `GET /api/print-driver/config` so deployment checks and a future local print service can see dry-run state, system-printer state, adapter kind, command-configured status, allowlist, and the fact that real physical dispatch is still unavailable. OpenAPI now validates at 53 paths / 142 schemas. Latest print job status polling update: print execution now has a first service-account-driven polling path in addition to direct driver callbacks. `server/printDriverAdapter.mjs` exposes `pollPrintJobStatus(...)`; guarded mode returns no physical-printer mutation, while dry-run mode can turn a sent `DRY-*` system-printer job into `printed` with `eventSource=driver_poll`. `server/apiServer.mjs` now exposes `POST /api/print-jobs/{printJobId}/poll-status` for a single job and `POST /api/print-jobs/status-poll` for batch polling queued / sent jobs. Both routes require `print.job.callback`, write driver-poll evidence through the same driver-status transition rules, and keep operation logs under `U-PRINT-DRIVER-A`. New validation `npm run print-job-status-polling:check` covers permission denial, dry-run dispatch to sent, batch poll to printed, persisted driver-poll metadata, service-account operation logs, and terminal no-op polling; PostgreSQL live now verifies the batch polling API updates real `print_jobs` and `operation_logs`. Latest print driver status update: print jobs now have a separate status polling and driver-callback API after dispatch. `server/apiServer.mjs` exposes `GET /api/print-jobs/{printJobId}` for polling the current job state and `POST /api/print-jobs/{printJobId}/driver-status` for driver callbacks or polling tasks to record `queued / sent / printed / failed / canceled`.
