The base bag / inventory style remains available as `底袋款式` in the detail panel so stock and production matching can still use `空白袋`. The durable prototype rule is recorded in `AGENTS.md`, the product decision is recorded in `DECISIONS.md`, `npm run build` passes, and the browser check at `http://127.0.0.1:5174/` verified the `美的空调` sample row plus the detail panel. QA screenshot: `screenshots/p0-order-entry-custom-print-type-1440x900.png`.

Latest development-planning update: `docs/development/code-development-plan.zh-CN.md` now defines the code development plan from the current P0 office prototype baseline through P0 scenario completion, frontend structure normalization, ERD/API drafting, real sample data, P1 page expansion, and later backend/persistence work. The plan keeps product requirements anchored to `docs/product/requirements.zh-CN.md` and documents build/browser verification expectations for each development stage.

Latest P0 hardening pass: the current untracked prototype workspace has been committed as a Git baseline on `main`, and active development is on `codex/p0-office-hardening`. The P0 office prototype now separates fixture data into `src/data/fixtures.js` and rule-based order parsing into `src/lib/orderParser.js`, leaving `src/App.jsx` focused on UI state and page behavior. Order entry now supports inline editing of parsed rows, including customer, product, size, color, handle, style, print flag, quantity, fulfillment, latest-needed time, inventory status, and estimated amount. The parser handles messy P0 samples such as `3038红500个`, `30*38 红500 黑100`, and `中号横款黑1000`. Saving and confirming parsed rows now creates local formal order lines, reserves available stock, creates fulfillment tasks where appropriate, and routes shortage rows into shared office todos. Fulfillment actions now update local state for quantity mismatch, unable-to-outbound, print/label preview, printed state, and express/LTL picked-up confirmation. Statement/payment actions now update send/payment/write-off state and create variance todos when underpaid. `npm run build` passes after these changes, and the running app was checked in the in-app browser at `http://127.0.0.1:5174/` across all six office pages. QA screenshots were saved to `screenshots/p0-office-hardening-1440x900.png` and `screenshots/p0-order-entry-inline-compact-1440x900.png`.

Latest finance/statement refinement: V1 finance now separates customer-send statements from internal archive statements. The system can start from a default Excel template, then adapt to the factory's real template later. Customer statements summarize this-period actual deliveries, chargeable quantity, free/non-chargeable quantity, adjustment amount, and final receivable, while internal details retain delivery batches, evidence, customer confirmations, debt carry-forward, payment matching, and internal notes. Statement send records keep channel, recipient, exported file, sender, sent time, collection-code inclusion, and customer replies/payment screenshots. Payment entry and payment confirmation are separate permissions; office/outbound/driver accounts can submit payment clues, but only payment-confirmation accounts can confirm arrival and write off receivables. The owner's mother / management mobile account can confirm payment. Routine scrap-sale income can be recorded as simple other income and does not enter customer statements or order revenue.

Latest external-processing refinement: external-processing / peer-processing print orders are service-only print orders. They use a separate external-processing print price table, bill by print unit price times actual printed quantity, and can preserve per-order override snapshots. Supplied material is only recorded as lightweight notes, roll count, optional photo, and optional estimated printable quantity; it does not enter raw-material inventory or material-cost allocation. The order retains side, print color, and color-count fields for statistics, but V1 does not implement complex automatic pricing. Completion creates a printing-service fulfillment record using normal delivery / pickup / express evidence. Quality outcomes are customer accepts, allowance, reprint, no compensation / no handling, or other. Associated-factory small-plate / manual supplemental printing gets monthly summary export only, not full accounts payable.

Latest master-data landing refinement: initial master-data import rules are now fixed. Customers can import with only a name and default price table 1 / cash or unset settlement. Inventory import requires the standard color catalog first; unknown colors and unknown sizes become pending-confirmation rows. Initial stock must mark counted versus estimated / pending review. Employee accounts import disabled until roles, machines, and permissions are checked. Machine capacity starts as low-confidence manual estimates. Price-table import is a high-risk full-table import that must pre-check version, effective time, reviewer, and changed values before approval. The first template generation batch is customers, colors / aliases, sizes / finished-goods styles, employees, machine capacity, and initial finished-goods inventory; suppliers and raw-material batches follow second.

Latest implementation-landing refinement: V1 development should start with page shells and fake data, while ERD/API contracts are drafted in parallel. The first office desktop sequence is shared todo workbench, order entry / recognition, order pool, inventory lookup, outbound / fulfillment, and statements / payments, followed by workshop, warehouse / outbound, driver, management mobile, and TV refinements. V1 stays single factory and single operational/accounting tenant, with warehouse modeling limited to zones and states. Trial imports and real data rehearsals use a test tenant first. The first realistic sample dataset should contain 20-50 desensitized orders covering in-stock and out-of-stock stock/common goods, custom print, express/LTL, delivery, pickup, under/overdelivery, and statement/payment variance. Dot-matrix outbound/pickup and delivery templates come before express/LTL labels and statement Excel template polish.

Latest P0 prototype scope refinement: P0 is limited to six desktop office pages: shared todo workbench, order entry / recognition, order pool, inventory lookup, outbound / fulfillment, and statements / payments. Mobile surfaces and the TV board are fake-data previews / placeholders in P0. Order recognition uses a large pasted-text input, rule-based local parsing, and editable parsed lines, without AI or external APIs. Inventory, reservations, outbound states, document / label previews, and statement/payment tables are simulated first. Acceptance uses the 20-50 desensitized sample orders to validate workflow and exception handling before real APIs, real printers, or final Excel templates.

Latest P0 sample-data refinement: P0 can start with 30 synthetic but business-realistic records before real desensitized orders are available. The fixtures should include 12 customers covering settlement cycles, debt, pickup, delivery, and express/LTL behavior; eight order categories including in-stock stock, out-of-stock stock, custom print, printed stock, external-processing print, pickup, delivery, and express/LTL; messy pasted-order strings for parser testing; fake inventory using the real key of size/model + color + handle type + finished-goods style + zone/state; confirmed bag-size prices from the 2026-07-03 price image; silk-screen pricing using manual single-sided 0.09 yuan, manual double-sided 0.13 yuan, and machine tiers 0.09 / 0.08 / 0.06 yuan at 3000 / 5000 / 10000 pieces; and role fixtures for office, management, production supervisor, warehouse, driver, silk-screen, bag-making, and packing.

Latest P0 interaction refinement: P0 office pages should use a narrow left navigation plus a top page title/action area, with high-density SaaS ERP / operational-table styling rather than large cards or large whitespace. The navigation exposes the six usable office pages first and keeps later modules as gray placeholders. Order entry uses a top pasted-text input plus editable parsed table rows and side panels for complex print details. Inventory uses green for available, yellow for reserved/occupied, red for shortage, and gray for pending/unavailable. Statements are customer-first with the customer list on the left and period orders/payment status on the right. Status colors are unified across modules: normal blue/green, pending yellow, exception red, completed muted green, and voided gray.

Latest P0 shared-todo refinement: the P0 office shared-todo workbench starts with eight todo types: order draft pending confirmation, shortage pending handling, quantity difference pending handling, label waiting print, express/LTL pickup confirmation, statement waiting generation, payment variance pending confirmation, and boss/management watch. Sorting is rush / today first, then red exceptions, then latest required time and waiting duration. Todos remain unclaimed in a shared pool and record the actual handler. The right panel shows customer, order tail, source, summary, key quantities, amount impact, notes, and recommended actions. Batch actions are limited to low-risk snooze, print preview, viewed, and label printing; money, inventory, fulfillment, and customer-commitment decisions stay one-by-one. P0 reminders are only red dots, count badges, and pinned list placement, with handled-today and latest-7-days views for handoff.

Latest P0 order-entry refinement: the P0 order-entry / recognition page uses a large top input area with only recognize, clear, and fill-sample actions. Recognition automatically splits one pasted message into multiple editable order lines when it contains multiple sizes, colors, print contents, or fulfillment methods. The parsed table shows customer, product / print content, size, color, handle, finished-goods style, print flag, quantity, fulfillment, latest needed time, inventory status, and estimated amount, while complex print artwork, print side/color, notes, price snapshot, and source evidence live in a right-side detail panel. Missing-field blocking is order-type-specific: stock orders require only core stock fields, while custom print orders require production-critical print fields. Confidence uses normal/yellow/red states. Footer actions are save draft, save and confirm, split order, and void draft; complete manual entries can save and confirm directly without duplicate review.

Latest P0 order-pool refinement: the P0 order pool remains separate from shared todos. It is for search, filtering, traceability, and status lookup, while the shared todo handles immediate actions. The default scope is latest-30-days incomplete orders plus today-completed orders, with an all-orders switch. The main list uses order-line rows with original-order grouping/filtering. Columns include order/line number, customer, product or print content, size, color, handle, quantity, order type, current status, fulfillment method, latest needed time, amount, and exception marks. Filters cover customer, status, order type, fulfillment, exception flag, date range, statement status, and debt/payment variance. Clicking opens a right-side drawer with status/actions and sections for detail, production, inventory, packing, fulfillment, statement, and operation history. List actions stay low-risk: view, copy, open detail, and void draft.

Latest P0 inventory-lookup refinement: the P0 inventory page uses the real inventory key of size/model + color + handle type + finished-goods style + zone/state. It defaults to available, reserved/occupied, and waiting-pickup locked stock, with pending-handling/pending-scrap collapsed. Quantity columns show in-stock, reserved, available, waiting-pickup locked, pending-handling, and estimated/pending-review flag. Shortage view shows shortage quantity, reference-only similar-stock hints, estimated replenishment/production suggestion, and copyable customer message. Similar colors/sizes never count as available or support one-click substitution. Inventory source summaries show counted, workshop-reported, packing-counted, waiting-pickup locked, and estimated stock. P0 only exposes start inventory correction; it does not directly edit total inventory.

Latest P0 outbound/fulfillment refinement: the P0 outbound / fulfillment office page uses tabs for all, self-pickup, driver delivery, and express/LTL, backed by one shared fulfillment dataset. The default view focuses on today-due, incomplete outbound/fulfillment, and pending-exception records, while historical completed records stay behind filters. Rows show customer, order/line tail, fulfillment method, goods / spec shorthand, quantity/package count, latest needed time, status, and note markers. The detail drawer shows contact/address, order-line details, inventory source, package/label/document preview, customer notes, office notes, and operation history. Initial actions are mark prepared, complete outbound/fulfillment, quantity mismatch, unable to outbound, print/label preview, and confirm express/LTL picked up. Quantity mismatch uses a fixed reason dialog and creates an office todo rather than inline quantity editing. Printing remains simulated in P0 with browser previews and printed / void-reprint states before real printer integration.

Latest P0 statements/payments refinement: the P0 statements / payments page uses a customer-first default view showing customers needing current-period statements, customers with unpaid variance / debt, and customers with payments waiting confirmation. Settled history stays behind filters. The left customer list shows customer name, settlement cycle, current receivable, debt/overdue marker, payment-confirmation marker, and last statement date. The right detail area is split into customer summary, current-period orders / line table, totals, adjustments, received amount, variance handling, and operation history. Customer-facing detail summarizes by order line, while internal drilldown keeps delivery batches, packages, pickup/delivery/express evidence, and payment records. Initial actions are statement preview, mark sent, record received amount, variance pending confirmation, confirm write-off, and export placeholder. Underpayments default to variance pending confirmation, not automatic write-off. P0 export remains a browser table plus simple Excel placeholder until the real statement template is provided.

Latest P0 implementation-landing refinement: P0 now shifts from requirements questioning to building the six desktop office prototype pages. The implementation should use frontend-local React state and fake data first: shared todo, order entry / recognition, order pool, inventory lookup, outbound / fulfillment, and statements / payments are clickable; later modules are disabled placeholders. Fake data starts around 30 orders, 12 customers, inventory, todos, packages, and statements. Recognition, inventory, reservations, outbound, statement, printing, export, and payment states are simulated locally, without database, real API, AI, payment interface, real printer, or final Excel template. Acceptance is scenario-based with at least 2-3 business cases per page, and the local dev server must be opened in the browser for desktop visual / interaction checks before handoff.

Latest P0 prototype implementation: the React prototype now implements the six desktop office pages as clickable local-state screens: shared todo, order entry / recognition, order pool, inventory lookup, outbound / fulfillment, and statements / payments. Later modules remain disabled placeholders in the left navigation. The prototype includes 30 order-line records, 12 customers, inventory, todos, fulfillment records, package / document previews, and statements. Local interactions cover order recognition, todo handling, order filtering, inventory correction placeholder, outbound prepared / complete / mismatch / print-preview / express pickup confirmation, and statement preview / sent / received amount / variance / write-off placeholders. `npm run build` passes, and the app was opened at `http://127.0.0.1:5173/` in the in-app browser with desktop visual checks across all six pages and interaction checks for order recognition, outbound quantity mismatch modal, and payment registration modal.

Latest customer-group automation refinement: V1 customer-group automation is frozen to manual handling. V1 only records group metadata, copied original text, screenshot uploads, manual review, and manual confirmation. It does not automatically read groups, create formal orders, or send replies. Customer profiles keep lightweight group fields and an automation candidate level: disabled, record group only, future pilot candidate, or automation forbidden. Later V2/V3 automation remains split into conversation archive reading and Enterprise WeChat employee-account sending agent modules, with fixed templates only, no promises of accepted orders / scheduled production / guaranteed stock, and pilots limited to 5-10 stable customers or self-operated store groups after the core ERP flow works.

The repository now contains a clean baseline for the ERP production board prototype. It was migrated out of the previous Codex conversation workspace into `/Users/xu/Documents/ERP`.

The app is a frontend-only Vite + React prototype. It uses local React state and static sample data; there is no backend, persistence layer, router, authentication, or real ERP/API integration yet.

Product requirements are being refined one question at a time. Recent accepted requirements cover after-sales / customer complaint records, internal responsibility tracing, non-blocking after-sales follow-up, after-sales statistics, non-blocking customer risk hints during order entry, stock shortage confirmation, inventory reservation, shortage-change chains, customer-locked replenishment quantities, opportunistic stock replenishment suggestions, exact stock matching where similar color/size inventory is only a reference hint and same-standard-color batch color differences are not managed in V1, lightweight finished-goods warehouse zones for plain stock versus printed stock with model/size grouping, recommended outbound locations in task details, optional actual outbound location, complete-outbound quantity defaulting to expected quantity with quantity differences routed through quantity-mismatch handling, mandatory office-side void-and-reprint behavior for changed outbound / delivery / pickup documents, with the common shortage-at-picking case scoped mainly to factory self-pickup, lightweight customer-confirmation records for accepted self-pickup quantity changes with configurable high-risk evidence triggers, three self-pickup shortage outcomes for canceling remainder / taking actual quantity and waiting for remainder / waiting until full quantity is ready, original-price-snapshot handling and non-auto-cancel follow-up reminders for later self-pickup remainder batches, temporary prepared-goods holding for full-quantity pickup waits, shared office todo handling and management review/watch escalation for overdue or high-risk self-pickup follow-ups, statement periods based on actual pickup/delivery date, and delivery drivers receiving packed goods plus matching documents, inventory source visibility, and non-forced FIFO outbound recommendations, formal outbound / pickup flow, outbound worker mobile task pool with optional warehouse/location detail and advance-preparation states, customer and office notes in outbound details with later preset extraction, quantity-mismatch and unable-to-outbound office exception handling, finance/payment permissions, customer-group statement/payment flows, conversation archive reading with payment OCR matching, a separate Enterprise WeChat employee-account sending-agent module, shared automation safety / operations requirements, customer-group recognized order draft review with side-by-side source / parsed-result comparison and order-type-specific missing-field rules, inventory snapshot versus formal-order reservation rules, shared fulfillment-pending task pool and reminder timing, manual stock-entry save actions, permission-controlled office outbound-note printing, restricted future high-trust automatic order entry, helper material-preparation task pools grouped by workshop/zone and machine time window with rough one-roll estimates plus the captured bag body / handle material formula where configured, single-action helper-confirmed machine-side material pickup without mandatory bag-making-worker scan confirmation, lightweight machine-side staging for 1-3 rolls and estimated allocation across small/rush orders, trigger-based leftover registration, cross-day machine-side leftover carryover, QR/barcode goods-card and raw-material label direction with two separate label templates, price-hidden per-package express / less-than-truckload waiting-pickup labels with package sequence printed by office and attached by packing workers, unified standard color catalog / color card with manually confirmed source-scoped customer/supplier/factory aliases and management-permission controls for edits/merges/deactivation, office-controlled V1 printing permissions, photo/OCR-assisted raw-material inbound verification with supplier-color alias mapping, kg-based fabric/handle cost snapshots excluding freight and 5cm handle-width handling, raw-material inbound / machine-context issue-outbound / leftover ledger direction for material-to-output and gross-margin attribution, and first size-based bag body / handle formula support for rough gross-profit estimation.

Latest refinement: express / less-than-truckload goods placed in the waiting-pickup area only reserve inventory and do not enter inventory deduction or statements until staff confirms they were picked up; next-day confirmation may backfill the actual pickup date to the previous evening when known.

Latest label refinement: express / less-than-truckload uses one price-hidden package label per package as the paper voucher for ERP package records, with shipping display name, package sequence, package ID, and barcode / QR code; shipping display name is required before printing labels for custom printed express / less-than-truckload orders but optional for self-pickup, driver delivery, and ordinary stock orders. Self-pickup and driver delivery continue using their existing paper notes while statements stay unified from ERP delivery records.

Latest express / less-than-truckload refinement: next-day pickup confirmation is an office shared todo with package-level status and batch actions; unpicked packages remain reserved, trigger reminders, and custom cancelled packages enter pending handling / pending scrap rather than returning to available stock. Scrap requires production supervisor or management confirmation, not boss approval, and responsibility tracing records clues without automatic payroll deduction.

Latest label-printing refinement: express / less-than-truckload package labels can be printed after actual package details or preprinted by the office from expected package count, but actual packing is authoritative; mismatched labels must be voided and reprinted with history. V1 only requires system voiding for unused paper labels and does not force physical recovery, photo proof, or signed destruction. Packing completion needs no extra physical label-attachment confirmation: valid preprinted / printed labels allow direct waiting-pickup state, while unprinted labels move through waiting-label-print and then enter waiting pickup only after office confirms the paper labels printed. Waiting-label-print reminders stay in the office shared pool: red-dot after 30 minutes, top of office todo before end of day / same-day pickup window, no default boss push. The office label-print queue should be workshop-first, carry workshop / machine / submitter context, normally print one workshop per batch, sort urgent / ships-today first, keep same-order packages adjacent, and support physical label handoff without system receipt confirmation, packing-worker label issue feedback, overtime label fallback / exceptions, preview, print-batch records, partial success, print exceptions, fixed reprint reasons, one active label per package, one V1 template, test print, and 20-label batch limit. V1 does not add a separate batch checklist / handoff sheet.

Latest packing/statement refinement: packing workers submit actual package details and actual quantities even when custom printed orders are short or slightly over; the system warns on below-order quantity but does not block packing completion. Orders now separate original ordered quantity, actual packed/delivered quantity, chargeable quantity, free / non-chargeable quantity, and variance reason, so inventory/fulfillment can follow actual quantity while statements can show gifts or non-charged overruns such as delivered 1005 but charged 1000. Normal in-range overdelivery can be marked gift / non-chargeable by office staff with a default reason; out-of-range free overdelivery enters non-blocking boss/management watch. Below-order quantity enters quantity-difference pending handling until office or management chooses customer acceptance, allowance, actual-quantity billing, stock supplemental shipment / fill shortage, custom small-plate manual supplemental printing, remake, or other handling. It blocks formal outbound/delivery/pickup/express picked-up confirmation and statement inclusion, but not packing completion. It uses the office shared todo pool, red-dot reminder after 30 minutes, same-day fulfillment top priority, and follow-up after 1 day. Stock/common-goods short quantity usually becomes supplemental shipment linked to the original order without duplicate revenue, while custom printed short quantity usually bills actual qualified quantity or uses allowance; only exceptional custom cases use exact blank stock plus small-plate manual silk-screen. Confirmed supplemental handling automatically creates the matching task, reserves exact stock where applicable, supports cancellation with inventory release, and completes through normal fulfillment evidence without mandatory second customer confirmation. Supplemental fulfillment documents show quantity but amount 0 or `not charged again`; statement delivery details show supplemental batches by actual delivery date, while statement summary aggregates them into the original order line. Quantity differences are responsibility clues first, not automatic payroll deductions.

Latest supplemental-cost refinement: supplemental / fill-shortage cost stays on the original order or original after-sales / complaint record. Stock supplemental shipment deducts finished-goods inventory and attributes cost to the original order's after-sales / supplemental cost. Custom small-plate manual supplemental printing uses this factory's blank-bag stock, while the small plate and manual print work are handled by another / associated factory at 15 CNY per plate, 0.10 CNY per piece for single-sided content, and 0.13 CNY per piece for double-sided same content. V1 records cost snapshots and fixed responsibility-source clues without automatically creating new receivables or payroll deductions.

Latest bag-cost refinement: the first bag calculation workbook has been captured as `docs/product/assets/bag-cost-calculation-2026-06-28.xlsx`. It defines bag body cost from width, height, gusset, fold, seam, GSM, and fabric unit price, plus handle cost from handle width, length, GSM, unit price, and handle count. Actual size is recorded as `width*height*gusset`. Normal/default body GSM is 78g. Stock/common-goods bags, laminated bags, printed stock/common-goods bags, and non-special custom silk-screen orders are confirmed to use fold 3cm and seam 2cm. Normal/default handle length is 38cm, handle raw-material width is 5cm, handle GSM is 60g, and each bag has 2 handles. The factory has 3 fabric suppliers with different prices, and handle prices also vary by supplier, so fabric supplier/batch and handle supplier/batch pricing must be selected and snapshotted separately. Bag body fabric roll width must exactly match the calculated body required width, with no automatic wider/near-width substitution. The current price-table sizes now have a documented actual-size / body-width / fabric-length table. `30*38` / `30*38*10` is a common customer/price-table alias whose actual production, inventory, and costing standard is `30*37*10`, so its body fabric width is 90cm and fabric length is 42cm. V1 cost estimates are marked excluding loss until loss rate can be calibrated from operational data, and special models require manual constant confirmation. This is documented as an internal cost / gross-margin / replenishment estimate formula, separate from customer-facing price tables and order price snapshots.

Latest material-cost refinement: gross-margin estimates now separate rough estimate cost, actual batch cost, and machine/task allocated cost. Before material issue, rough estimates use the latest confirmed inbound price for the same spec or an office-selected supplier price, with source visibly marked. After raw-material label scanning in material issue/outbound mode, the backend can automatically use the inbound batch cost snapshot for that roll/batch. If a roll is shared across orders or replenishment tasks, the batch price is actual but per-order consumption may still be allocated by machine/task/output/formula/leftover data and marked as allocated rather than exact.

Latest replenishment refinement: stock replenishment now uses exact inventory keys, not only size/color. Blank minimum / target stock values do not trigger automatic warnings. Available stock is calculated as in-stock minus reserved, waiting-pickup locked, and pending-handling / scrap quantities. Suggested replenishment quantity starts from target-stock shortage plus customer-confirmed waiting quantity, then shows a rough one-roll output hint. Customer-locked quantities are protected first when customer orders and stock replenishment are merged into one production run. Priority is customer already waiting, urgent stock shortage, below minimum stock, dynamic sales suggestion, and opportunistic finish-the-roll production. Slow-moving 30-day low-sales items do not replenish unless below minimum stock, and then only near minimum stock. The daily 4 PM replenishment list enters the office / production-management shared todo pool with confirm, ignore, and remind-later actions; ignore requires a reason.

Latest raw-material workflow refinement: V1 raw-material handling now uses one QR/barcode per fabric roll or handle roll/piece, even when multiple rolls are listed under one raw-material supplier delivery-note line. Raw-material locations stay coarse: raw-material warehouse, workshop temporary storage, machine-side, and leftover area plus notes. OCR/photo recognition only prefills inbound drafts from incoming raw-material supplier notes or material labels; failed recognition or raw-material delivery-note/physical-material mismatch stays pending and does not create available inventory. These notes are separate from finished-goods outbound / customer delivery notes. Office staff can enter inbound documents, but confirmed kg prices, amounts, and cost snapshots require purchasing / cost / raw-material-price permission to correct. Material issue defaults to full-roll/full-piece movement to machine-side, with partial quantities only when actually split, weighed, metered, or clearly ratioed. Ink and auxiliary materials stay lightweight in V1 as basic inventory / notes without automatic deduction or order-level cost allocation. Scanning starts with USB/Bluetooth scanner keyboard-input mode plus mobile scan, not dedicated PDA devices.

Latest helper material-preparation refinement: helper material-preparation is now mostly confirmed. V1 initializes 3 workshop zones as machines 1-3, 4-6, and 7-9, but workshop-machine-helper bindings are configurable. Helpers see only their assigned workshop by default; office / production supervisor / management can view all, and temporary support can be authorized with audit. Preparation tasks are generated only from published schedules, not drafts, and are grouped by today / tomorrow, morning / afternoon / current batch, workshop, and machine. The system may recommend or reserve roll IDs, but the helper's actual scanned roll is authoritative. One action, machine-side material pickup, moves material to machine-side state; bag-making workers do not scan-confirm. If schedules change after material is already at machine side, it enters machine-side pending handling for keep / move / return decisions. Machine-side rolls older than one day without linked current or tomorrow tasks enter review reminders only.

Latest cost-closed-loop refinement: the accepted flow for more accurate bag costing is inbound cost snapshot, raw-material label, material issue scan, production reporting / leftover registration, cost allocation, and order gross-margin snapshots. Orders should keep estimated margin, batch-cost margin, and allocated margin as separate versions instead of overwriting one number. Estimated margin supports order-taking / quotation / scheduling judgment, batch-cost margin corrects supplier and batch price variance after issue scanning, and allocated margin supports production review, month-end analysis, machine loss review, and material-efficiency analysis.

Latest accounting-control refinement: cost and gross-margin results should be system-generated with minimal manual intervention. Staff may change only source prices, specifically bag sales price versions / order price snapshots and raw-material price changes or corrections. Calculated cost, margin, allocation, and loss results are not directly editable; corrections create new snapshots or variance records with audit history.

Latest material-margin refinement: V1 gross margin means material gross margin only, excluding labor, electricity, rent, equipment depreciation, and indirect costs. Negative estimated material margin triggers a strong warning and low margin triggers a yellow warning, but order entry is not automatically blocked. Free overdelivery uses chargeable quantity for revenue and actual produced / packed / delivered quantity for material cost, so unpaid extra pieces reduce margin. Stock/common-goods inventory can use weighted-average material cost or the latest available cost by exact finished-goods inventory key rather than tracing every finished bag to a roll. Shared-roll allocation should use size-based material formula times actual qualified quantity, not simple piece count. Returned / transferred leftover value is calculated from the original roll cost and remaining weight / meters / ratio and subtracts from task cost. Special-model costs remain `cost pending confirmation / rough estimate` until constants are confirmed.

Latest small-plate accounting refinement: V1 records associated-factory plate / manual-print fees as original-order internal supplemental cost and supports monthly export, but does not build full accounts payable yet. Manual-print cost is calculated by actual qualified supplemental print quantity, while fulfillment records the final customer-supplemented quantity. Single/double-side unit cost is auto-filled from the original order and can be overridden with reason. Historical supplemental-printing tasks keep their cost snapshots after later price changes. Customer-paid extra supplemental amounts are recorded as original-order extra-payment adjustments.

Latest responsibility refinement: V1 responsibility tracing records responsible stage, optional responsible person, notes, reference loss, and handling history, but never auto-deducts employee pay. Management / performance-confirmation permission is required to confirm whether to deduct and the manual deduction amount. Multiple responsible people are supported, reference losses are advisory only, employee mobile does not show real-time deduction notices, and responsibility / deduction confirmation does not block order or statement flow.

Latest reporting/loss refinement: quantity reporting recognizes qualified finished goods only. Normal reporting does not require scrap/loss entry; abnormal loss is captured only when shortages, excessive overrun, obvious defective bags, printing problems, customer complaints, or inventory/packing/outbound differences matter. The factory currently bags routine bag-making waste and sells it as scrap, with no dedicated per-order scrap handler, so V1 should not introduce a formal scrap workflow for ordinary waste. Optional scrap-bag / waste-batch records can capture date, optional workshop/machine, estimated weight or bag count, handler, scrap-sale amount, optional buyer, and notes, but they do not affect customer revenue, statements, or finished-goods inventory. Problem goods that affect orders, inventory, amount handling, quality complaints, or responsibility judgment enter pending handling / pending scrap. Responsibility clues are generated only for customer, amount, inventory, or performance-relevant issues and never auto-deduct pay. Daily machine-counter readings stay optional telemetry until the counter rules are verified on site.

Latest payroll refinement: current payroll uses punch-clock attendance hours with formula `hours * (hourly base wage + hourly position subsidy) + performance`. Example: 300 May hours for a female bag-making machine worker uses `300 * (10 female-worker base wage + 5 bag-machine position subsidy) + 300 performance = 4800 CNY`. Employee profiles maintain each employee's hourly base wage and default / current position; position records maintain hourly position subsidies. Payroll drafts use effective-dated snapshots for employee base wage, position, and subsidy so later changes do not rewrite settled months or historical drafts. Temporary position changes in V1 are handled manually by HR during payroll review with notes, not automatically split by daily production task, until the existing payroll spreadsheet confirms whether finer rules are needed. Except for silk-screen machine workers, current employees normally punch four times per day, mapped to morning clock-in, morning clock-out, afternoon clock-in, and afternoon clock-out; standard times are fixed but configurable by role / employee, with exact ordinary-employee punch times to be configured from the current schedule or payroll spreadsheet. 12:00-13:00 is unpaid lunch/rest time. Silk-screen machine workers default to two punches per day, clock-in and clock-out. ERP should evaluate missing punches by role / employee attendance rule, preserve extra raw punches while matching valid segment records, and support same-day temporary leave / return segments such as a 14:00 punch-out and 16:00 return. Early punch-out for temporary leave requires prior verbal permission from management or prior leave/explanation in the large group. Payroll hours are accumulated from valid work segments at minute precision, so the away segment is excluded from punch-clock wage hours by default unless HR confirms a special paid-hour adjustment; payroll export should display hours with 2 decimals by default. V1 temporary-leave reasons use fixed options, and HR only needs lightweight evidence flags such as verbally told management or explained in the large group unless a case is high-risk or disputed. Late arrival, early leave, and half-day leave are allowed and tracked statistically, not extra automatic deductions. Overtime has no premium; large-group-notified late work counts as normal punch-clock hours. Payroll cycle is the natural month, HR reviews the previous month from the 1st to 5th of the following month, and payroll is released after review. The existing Deli punch-clock machine is confirmed from a field screenshot as D5FN, with only masked SN stored in long-lived docs, and it appears online in Deli e+. Deli's official cloud open documentation lists DL-D5FN as a supported comprehensive sign-in API model, so API sync is feasible in principle, but the default path remains attendance-detail Excel / CSV import first; automatic Deli cloud / open-platform synchronization waits for account permission and App-Key / App-Secret credentials. A dedicated HR / personnel role handles attendance exceptions, missed punches, make-up punches, abnormal punch records, employee matching exceptions, and payroll review reminders. Missed-punch / make-up requests are initiated by the employee or supervisor with a reason; routine cases are handled by HR, while repeated, unusual-time, cross-day, monthly make-up count above threshold, or schedule/reporting-conflict cases require supervisor confirmation or management review. HR does not get performance / deduction final-confirmation permission by default, and performance is manually entered/imported in V1. The user will provide the existing payroll spreadsheet later; until then V1 should prepare source data, import/export summaries, and payroll drafts rather than final automatic payroll release.

Latest attendance-integration target: the final payroll-attendance workflow must be direct ERP synchronization from the confirmed Deli D5FN device, not routine manual export from Deli and upload into ERP. Workers punch on the physical device; ERP pulls Deli punch records automatically or through an HR `sync attendance` action and displays punches, exceptions, make-up-punch items, hour summaries, and payroll drafts inside ERP. Excel / CSV import remains only a transition and emergency fallback for missing API permission, API outage, historical backfill, or reconciliation.

Attendance platform decision: Deli is the first provider to attempt because the factory already owns a D5FN device, but the ERP must not be locked to Deli. If Deli cannot provide acceptable open API access, pricing / contract terms, data completeness, sync reliability, history backfill, or employee / punch-record synchronization, the business should replace it with another open-API attendance platform. ERP should keep one normalized attendance model across providers: raw punch records, employee mapping, device/source metadata, sync batches, exceptions, manual corrections, hour summaries, and payroll drafts.

Enterprise WeChat attendance direction: the factory already uses Enterprise WeChat, so attendance-provider evaluation should prioritize Enterprise WeChat-compatible physical devices or Enterprise WeChat check-in / hardware check-in APIs. Enterprise WeChat can serve identity / address-book matching and notifications, but ERP remains the employee-facing attendance UI. Employees punch on the device, then use ERP mobile `My attendance` to view only their own punch records, exceptions, make-up-punch status, monthly hours, and payroll-review summary; employees cannot edit raw punch records or view other employees' attendance.

Latest scheduling refinement: bag-making scheduling is assigned to concrete machines, dates, and sequences, not minute-level start/end times for every task. Draft schedules are visible only to office / production management; published schedules appear on workshop mobile and the large TV board, and published changes keep audit history. Published rush insertion directly changes the queue, records before/after sequence and reason, and auto-refreshes workshop mobile / TV. The large TV board shows scannable fields only: current task, next task, customer / replenishment mark, size-color-handle, planned / completed / remaining quantity, rough estimated completion, and exception marks. Initial capacity is manually maintained by machine + size/model as rough daily/hourly output; missing values fall back to defaults and are marked low confidence. Estimated completion is a reference calculated from queue sequence, quantity / default capacity, mold-change buffer, material-wait buffer, and rest / end-of-day impact, shown coarsely as expected morning / afternoon / tonight / tomorrow or a rough window. Manual ETA edits require a reason, and late estimates enter office / production-management todos; serious same-day delivery risk enters management watch. Formal mold-change work orders are created only when technician coordination is actually needed. Cross-day unfinished tasks stay on the original machine queue or continue-task area with completed/remaining quantity.

Latest silk-screen refinement: V1 silk-screen uses a same-day task pool with a tomorrow preview. Marked / claimed tasks are not automatically released if not started; workers, office staff, or supervisors can cancel or reassign, and the system only reminds. At least one print photo is required after setup, with more photos allowed for double-sided, different-content, multi-color, or high-risk jobs. Actual printed quantity is reported by the silk-screen worker and feeds later loss / variance analysis. Plates / screens are treated as mostly one-time consumables without inventory management, and ink is not deducted from inventory in V1. External-processing / peer-processing print orders are separate print-only tasks that do not enter bag-making or finished-goods inventory.

Latest silk-screen exception / external-processing statement refinement: silk-screen print photos can be returned for retake with old/new photo history, return reason, and override support. Silk-screen exceptions split into continue and pause-for-confirmation paths with fixed reasons. External-processing print jobs commonly use one layout and several customer-supplied rolls; V1 does not manage leftover material or leftover return, and the main quantity is actual printed quantity. External-processing print receivables use print unit price times actual printed quantity with price snapshots, statement fields focused on print service, and quality issues routed to after-sales / complaints.

Latest bag-making reporting refinement: bag-making workers must tap start once for each task so the system records start time, machine, operator, task, and schedule context, but forgotten starts can be backfilled at completion and flagged as missing normal start records. Bag-making completion auto-routes by task type without per-task office confirmation; below-plan completion enters quantity-difference / exception handling without blocking the next task. Workers may skip to the next task when needed, but must choose a reason such as waiting for material, mold change not completed, rush job inserted, machine issue, supervisor arrangement, or other.

Latest bag-making exception refinement: bag-making exceptions split into report-and-continue versus report-and-pause-for-confirmation. V1 uses fixed reasons such as printing, material, machine, size / mold, quantity, unclear customer / order information, and other. Exceptions enter a shared production management / office pool; production management handles on-site action while office records customer / amount / statement / after-sales impact. Ordinary exceptions do not notify boss by default, while serious or repeated issues enter boss / management watch. Every exception closes with a clear outcome such as continue production, change sequence, wait for material / repair, route to quantity difference, route to after-sales / quality issue, or cancel / void task.

Latest bag-making photo and cross-day refinement: bag-making mobile only shows published tasks for the current machine / current schedule plus cross-day continue tasks, with temporary substitutions driven by schedule records. Custom printed orders require a finished-goods photo after a qualified sample appears; blank stock replenishment does not require one by default. Returned or unclear finished-goods photos stay out of the customer-notification-ready pool until retaken / accepted, with old and new photos plus return reason preserved. Completed quantity is submitted by the current logged-in actual bag-making worker, and short or excessive quantities are flagged by variance rules without blocking the next task. Cross-day tasks require same-day qualified quantity entry before ending the day, and the next day shows yesterday's completed and remaining quantity.

Latest customer-notification refinement: finished-goods photos are required per custom printed order line, and unclear / wrong photos can be returned for retake with history. After a valid finished-goods photo is uploaded, the line enters a shared waiting-to-notify pool. V1 customer notification is human-led: ERP generates copyable text and brings up the photo, office / management manually sends it through WeChat / Enterprise WeChat / private chat, then confirms in ERP. This status means customer notified only, not packed or shipped. Customer-group auto-send remains later whitelist-only assistance, not the V1 main flow.

Latest packing-task refinement: packing tasks appear only when packing is actually needed. Custom printed orders and printed stock/common-goods normally enter packing, while blank stock/common-goods enter packing only when the order, carrier method, customer notes, office instruction, or later fulfillment requires it. V1 does not require a separate start-packing action; packing workers submit real package details and `packing completed`. Packing exceptions use fixed reasons and go to the office shared pool, with office / authorized accounts handling customer contact, order changes, label reprints, quantity-difference routing, or after-sales routing.

Latest office-workbench refinement: V1 office operational todos default to a shared office pool visible to office clerks, owner-mother / management mobile accounts, and production management / supervisor, with the actual handler recorded when someone processes an item. The workbench prioritizes same-day or flow-blocking items such as shipment / pickup due today, quantity differences, waiting label print, waiting customer notification, inventory shortages, production exceptions, after-sales / complaints, and price approval. Ordinary todos stay as workbench / red-dot reminders; boss review is reserved for material issues like price approval, large allowance, overdue debt, serious production exceptions, repeated complaints, out-of-range free overdelivery, or overstock-risk replenishment. Manual complete order entry can use save-and-confirm, while automation-created drafts still require review. Todos support snooze / remind later.

Latest review-threshold refinement: V1 default boss-review and reminder thresholds are confirmed. Newly created debt does not immediately enter boss review; debt enters boss review when more than 7 days past billing cycle and over 2000 CNY, or when total debt exceeds 10000 CNY. Ordinary debt only warns; direct order acceptance is blocked only by the explicit customer state `management confirmation required before order acceptance`. Rounding, write-offs, discounts, and allowances enter boss review at over 5% or over 100 CNY, without blocking payment or statements. Free overdelivery uses the reasonable-overproduction thresholds of over 50 pieces below 5000 ordered pieces or over 100 pieces at 5000+ ordered pieces. Price approval reminds 2 hours before planned effectiveness and does not auto-apply if unapproved. Inventory adjustments enter boss review above 500 pieces or 300 CNY estimated value. Serious production exceptions enter boss review when they affect same-day fulfillment, involve compensation/allowance, repeat twice for the same machine or employee in one day, or exceed 300 CNY estimated loss. Ordinary todos use 30-minute red dots, same-day pinning, and follow-up after 1 day unresolved.

Latest customer-profile refinement: customer profiles now separate customer entity from contacts. A customer can have multiple contacts with roles such as owner, finance, family, worker, third-party pickup person, and third-party print worker. Each customer normally has at most one business order group recorded as metadata, with group-name history and manual correction, not as technical WeChat / Enterprise WeChat integration in V1. Customer notes are split into customer-visible, office internal, finance, and packing / fulfillment preference notes. Customer risk states are normal, watch, management confirmation required before order acceptance, and order suspended; watch hints do not block orders, while the two high-risk states require customer-risk handling or management permission. Orders and fulfillment records preserve confirmed snapshots so later customer-profile edits do not rewrite history.

Latest order-recognition refinement: order recognition drafts now preserve original source messages, files/images/OCR results, source customer or group, sender, sent time, and source message ID. The draft review page uses side-by-side source-versus-parsed layout, parsed fields carry high/medium/low confidence, suspected duplicates are flagged but not automatically voided, customer follow-up modifications create explicit modification records, manual corrections can become future hints, and inventory/prices are rechecked before a formal order is created because draft values are only recognition-time snapshots.

Latest order-structure refinement: one customer message / customer order creates one original order by default, with independent order lines / sub-orders underneath. Lines split by size, color, handle type, print / non-print status, print content, finished-goods style, or fulfillment method. Original order numbers preserve the customer's one-time order context, while production, inventory, packing, fulfillment, exceptions, and statements operate primarily by order line. Lines can flow independently, and customer statements show line rows grouped by the same original order number with totals.

Latest data-model refinement: V1 core data now centers on original orders plus order lines. Orders and fulfillment documents keep customer/contact/address/fulfillment snapshots. Finished-goods inventory is keyed by size/model, color, handle type, finished-goods style, and warehouse zone/state, with on-hand, reserved, available, waiting-pickup locked, and pending-handling/scrap quantities. Packages, delivery records, workshop reports, price snapshots, statements, payments, and after-sales/exceptions all link back to order lines where needed. Payroll, deep cost accounting, and automation bots remain later extensions rather than first-core tables.

Latest API/database boundary refinement: V1 implementation boundaries are confirmed. Original ordered, workshop-reported, packed, delivered, and chargeable quantities belong to separate source records and must not overwrite each other. Inventory changes go through ledger entries instead of direct total edits. Key actions write operation logs. Photos/files use one attachment table linked by business type and ID. Price snapshots are immutable against later price-table changes. Outbound notes, delivery notes, pickup notes, and package labels are versioned with void/reprint history. Operational todos share one todo table with type-specific handling. The first API phase covers main flows only: order drafts/formal orders, inventory lookup/reservation/release, scheduling, workshop reporting, packing, outbound/delivery, statements/payments, attachments, and logs.

Latest ERD implementation refinement: V1 ERD landing rules are confirmed. Business documents use readable numbers plus internal primary keys; customer names are not globally unique and repeated contacts/phones only trigger duplicate prompts; finished-goods inventory uniqueness is standard size/model, factory standard color, handle type, finished-goods style, warehouse zone, and inventory state; attachments link by business type, business ID, and purpose; formal records are voided/closed/replaced instead of hard-deleted; key records keep both business occurred time and system recorded time; Excel imports pre-check errors and duplicates before users choose skip/merge/insert; operation logs focus on money, inventory, production, fulfillment, responsibility, and audit-impacting actions rather than ordinary view-only activity.

Latest acceptance-test refinement: V1 launch acceptance scenarios are confirmed. The minimum scenario set covers order recognition / manual natural-language entry, in-stock stock/common-goods reservation, out-of-stock multi-line stock order handling, custom printed full flow, quantity variance for overdelivery and underdelivery, pickup/delivery shortage with old-note voiding and dot-matrix reprint, express / less-than-truckload package labels and pickup confirmation, and statements by actual delivery date with payment variance handling. Acceptance records should keep test data, operator/account, expected result, actual result, and failed items should enter a fix list.

Latest module-progress refinement: `docs/product/module-confirmation-progress.zh-CN.md` now has stricter anti-duplication rules. Confirmed module rules must not be re-asked under another module name. For modules at 90%+ confirmation, only implementation materials such as page fields, button permissions, initial data, thresholds, templates, devices, real spreadsheets, API, and ERD should be asked. The finished-goods inbound / loose inventory / machine-side direct outbound module is now treated as 88% confirmed: the main flow is fixed, and only implementation configuration remains.

Latest project-management refinement: module confirmation progress is now tracked in `docs/product/module-confirmation-progress.zh-CN.md`. It separates confirmed modules from unresolved question pools so future requirement discussions can avoid repeating already settled details and can batch remaining questions by module.

Latest page/permission refinement: V1 surfaces are now confirmed as office desktop, management mobile, large TV production board, workshop mobile, warehouse / outbound mobile, and driver mobile. The office desktop uses a shared todo workbench plus a separate order pool; production management has its own workbench; the TV board is read-only; role defaults can be extended with account-level special permissions. Driver delivery evidence should use ERP in-app camera capture to generate ERP-owned watermark photos, with external watermark-camera photos only as a transition fallback.

Latest page-entry refinement: V1 page entries are now confirmed and should not be re-asked. Office desktop starts at the shared todo workbench and uses main navigation for order entry, order pool, scheduling, inventory, outbound / fulfillment, packing / labels, statements / payments, customers, price tables, and master data. Management mobile is lightweight only: pending review / approval, price review, large allowance, serious exceptions, debt / overdue debt, and production progress. Workshop mobile routes accounts directly to role task pools. Warehouse / outbound mobile stays short-flow with waiting pick / outbound, prepared goods, quantity mismatch, unable outbound, and inventory lookup. Driver mobile covers today's delivery tasks, detail, watermark photo, optional signature photo, and completion. The first implementation should build page shells and fake-data flows before real APIs.

Latest office-todo workbench refinement: the office shared-todo page now has confirmed page behavior. It uses a compact grouped left list plus a right detail panel, shows type, customer, order tail number, goods / spec shorthand, wait / latest-needed time, and urgent / exception marks in each row, exposes actions by todo type, keeps todos unassigned, records the actual handler, limits batch actions to low-risk label printing and snooze, and keeps a same-day handled view.

Latest button-permission refinement: the V1 first-pass button matrix is confirmed. Office handles order creation / confirmation / edits and unproduced-order voids; office and production management handle scheduling actions; workshop accounts only start tasks, upload photos, report quantities / exceptions, and pause / continue; outbound accounts complete outbound or report quantity / outbound issues without editing order or finance data; inventory adjustments need confirmation permission to take effect; printing, payment confirmation, and performance / deduction confirmation are account-level special permissions; office can create after-sales records while responsibility and deduction confirmation stay permission-controlled.

Latest order-entry page refinement: manual order entry and pasted recognition should share one natural-language parse-and-review interaction. Manual entry is not primarily dropdown-by-dropdown; the office clerk types the full phone / on-site / private-chat / verbal order into a blank input area, ERP parses it into editable order lines below, and fallback dropdown fields are used only for missing or low-confidence fields. The draft review line table, actions, risk hints, and recheck-before-formal-order behavior are now confirmed.

Latest warehouse-outbound page refinement: the warehouse / outbound mobile task pool fields are confirmed. Tasks group by waiting pick / outbound, prepared goods, quantity mismatch, and unable to outbound. Rows use two compact lines with fulfillment method, customer, order tail, goods / spec shorthand, quantity, and urgent / exception marks. Sorting prioritizes same-day, urgent, and latest-needed time. Details show customer / fulfillment / phone / notes / latest time, line details, recommended zone / location, and inventory source. Inventory marks distinguish trusted and pending stock, `pending handling` cannot be normal outbound stock, quantity mismatch uses fixed reasons with optional photos, and outbound staff can lightly mark goods as prepared.

Latest workshop-task page refinement: workshop mobile task detail fields are confirmed for silk-screen, bag-making, helper material preparation, packing, and technician mold-change. Lists show task type, machine, sequence, order tail, customer or replenishment mark, size, color, handle, quantity, latest time, and urgent / exception marks. Each role detail page shows only role-relevant fields and actions; all share one exception entry with role-specific reasons and continue / pause handling. Cross-day tasks pin the next day with yesterday completed quantity, remaining quantity, and continue-today context, while bag-making cross-day tasks require daily qualified quantity.

Latest driver-delivery page refinement: driver mobile delivery task details are confirmed. Driver states are waiting delivery, in delivery, completed, and delivery exception. Lists use compact two-line rows and follow office / outbound manual delivery sequence before fallback sorting. Details show customer, contact, phone, full address, navigation, delivery note number, packages, order-line summary, customer notes, and office notes. Drivers tap confirm loaded after checking packed goods against the document, report loading / delivery exceptions back to warehouse or office, and complete delivery with a required ERP watermarked photo, optional signature photo, automatic driver/time/location recording, fixed exception reasons, and optional duplicate paper-note status.

Latest master-data refinement: V1 initial master data should use Excel import plus backend editing for customer profiles, factory standard colors and aliases, standard size / finished-goods style specs, machine capacity baselines, employee accounts, initial finished-goods inventory, suppliers, and lightweight raw-material batch records. Machine capacity starts with manual rough values and should later be calibrated from the user's production summary sheet showing daily model, speed, and output for machines 1-9 from the start of the year to the current date.

Latest master-data template refinement: V1 initial master-data workbook structures are confirmed. Customer data imports through customer entity, contacts / phones / addresses, and customer notes / preferences sheets. Color data imports through standard color and source-scoped color alias sheets. Size and style data imports through size spec and finished-goods style sheets with allowed-size validation. Initial finished-goods inventory imports exact inventory keys. Machine capacity, employee accounts, suppliers, and raw-material batches each have first-pass fields, with raw-material batches preserving supplier original color, factory standard color, width, GSM, kg, CNY/kg, freight-included flag, inbound date, batch / roll number, and photo/OCR source.

Latest master-data import refinement: V1 initial imports should first run in a test tenant or test environment before formal import. Incomplete customers can import with only a customer name, defaulting to price table 1 and cash / unset settlement until office staff completes details. Standard colors should exist before inventory import; unknown colors enter pending confirmation and are not available stock. Unknown sizes become special-model pending-confirmation rows and do not feed pricing, inventory, scheduling, costing, or replenishment automatically. Initial inventory can be counted or estimated / pending review, and the UI must show that trust level. Imported employee accounts are disabled until an admin checks roles, machines, and permissions. Machine capacity starts as manual estimates until the production summary sheet calibrates it. Ordinary master data can partially import valid rows with failed-row reports, while inventory, price, and permission imports require full pre-check and confirmation before taking effect.

Latest print-template refinement: V1 print/export scope is confirmed as outbound / self-pickup notes, delivery notes, express / less-than-truckload package labels, and customer statement Excel export. Pickup and outbound can share one two-copy template, delivery uses a separate template, non-print orders hide print unit price, handle type appears in line items, changed printed documents must be voided and reprinted, and express / less-than-truckload package labels remain price-hidden. Formal outbound / pickup / delivery notes should use the factory's existing dot-matrix printer and paper, with high-density tabular layouts, print preview, dynamic fields by order type, optional paper note number, merge printing only for same customer / same fulfillment method / same batch, statement Excel split into statement summary and delivery details, and template editing restricted to office / admin / template-maintenance permission accounts.

Latest field-permission refinement: field editing now depends on both account permission and order stage. Office staff can edit confirmed orders within stage limits; production-critical and finance-critical fields are separated; warehouse workers report quantity mismatch rather than editing order quantity; workshop workers cannot edit customer order fields; management mobile confirmations are visible only with the matching permission; key-field changes retain before/after values, operator, time, reason, and note/label void-and-reprint linkage.

Latest state-machine refinement: original orders now roll up child order-line states, while order lines own the real business state. Business state and exception tags stay separate, order lines can carry multiple parallel todos, delivered lines are corrected only through additive records rather than rollback, key state changes require logs, auto-transitions require explicit actions, and cancel / close states keep distinct reasons such as customer cancellation, stock-unavailable cancellation, duplicate void, recognition-error void, and management rejection.

Latest state-dictionary refinement: the first V1 status dictionary is confirmed. Original orders use roll-up states only. Order lines use main business states from draft / pending confirmation through scheduling, silk-screen, bag-making, packing, outbound / fulfillment, delivered, and closed. Exception tags such as missing information, insufficient inventory, quantity variance, production exception, photo retake required, waiting customer confirmation, after-sales in progress, and responsibility pending confirmation are additive rather than replacing the main state. Production tasks, packing tasks, packages, fulfillment records, statements, payments, after-sales records, and responsibility records each have first-pass state dictionaries.

Latest finished-goods inventory refinement: V1 inventory locations start with coarse zones plus model/size grouping and notes, not detailed shelf/layer/bin locations. Finished-goods status separates warehouse-counted, workshop-reported / loose, reserved, waiting-pickup locked, and pending-handling / scrap stock. V1 stocktaking uses cycle counts and permission-confirmed corrections rather than forced full-warehouse monthly counts. Safety stock is configured by exact inventory key, replenishment creates suggestions first, and every stock movement must generate an inventory ledger entry.

Latest machine-side direct outbound refinement: blank stock / loose workshop-reported goods can be dispatched directly from the machine as a transition exception, but the flow must link to a customer and formal order/order line; if no ERP order exists, office staff creates a quick stock order first. Warehouse outbound staff are the preferred confirmer, while office or production supervisor can proxy-enter when warehouse staff are absent; bag-making workers only report production quantity. Machine-side direct outbound creates production-report and outbound/fulfillment records together, supports electronic confirmation first with later back-print / document linking, immediately offsets the day's workshop-reported / machine-side output, and subtracts already dispatched quantities when the task is finally completed. Same-day pending review, red-dot after day-end, and one-day follow-up reminders prevent missed records. Variances use inventory adjustment / cycle-count correction and may create responsibility clues, but never auto-deduct pay.

## Known Constraints

- The UI currently has a desktop-first minimum width.
- Several actions simulate state changes only in memory.
- Narrow desktop hides the right detail panel, so some actions become less discoverable.
- Small text density is intentional for the prototype but remains a usability risk for factory-floor displays.
- The original WeChat temporary image for the bag price table was no longer present; its extracted pricing rules are preserved in documentation.

## Immediate Next Step

Use `docs/product/requirements.zh-CN.md` as the Chinese source of truth, continue grouped requirement refinement, and fold accepted answers back into the product docs plus `DECISIONS.md` when durable. Cost-related business rules are now mostly confirmed for V1; next cost work should collect real constants and calibration data for special models / new processes, loss rate, raw-material prices, roll length, leftovers, and low-margin thresholds.
